# VueOneMapper — Autonomous Loop Brief

**For Claude Code agents running on `/loop` or autonomously.** Read this *before* doing anything. Update `## Status` at the bottom every iteration so the next loop run knows where you stopped.

---

## End goal

A C# generator that turns a VueOne digital-twin `Control.xml` into a complete EAE 24.1 IEC 61499 project for the SMC rig (M262 + M580 + BX1) and the simulator. Change the digital twin, click one button, the new layout drives the real PLCs. No engineer hand-writes IEC 61499 for each layout change.

## Current focus — RIG (M262 + M580 + BX1); the Test Simulator path was REMOVED

The Test Simulator path was **deleted** (2026-06-16): `MainForm_simulator.cs`,
`CodeGen/Services/SimulatorPostProcessor.cs`, and
`MapperTests/SimulatorEndToEndHarness.cs` no longer exist. MapperUI has one
generation button — **Test Runtime** (`btnTestStation1_Click`) — which generates
the full 3-PLC rig project (M262 Feed + M580 Assembly/Disassembly + BX1 covers,
MQTT on BX1). `SimulatorFullSystem` survives only as inert config (never set
true); `SimulatorRecipeMode` is still LIVE — the `Data → State-Transition Table`
menu preview sets it.

**The live, dated work record is `## Status` below** — read the top bullets for
the actual current state (recent: MQTT on BX1, covers folded into the M580
Assembly/Disassembly flow, and conservative dead-code cleanup). The
behaviour-preserving gate is the byte-identical generated-Demonstrator diff
(`_gate/`, golden under `C:\_gate`); `MapperTests` runs 199 active tests.

**Standing constraints (still in force):**
- ❌ DO NOT touch MQTT, recipes, HCF, sysres/syslay, CATs, or Control.xml parsing without an explicit task + the byte-identical gate.
- ❌ DO NOT regenerate the hand-crafted `MapperTests\TestData\SMC_Rig_IO_Bindings.xlsx`.
- ❌ DO NOT touch `C:\Demonstrator` directly — the Mapper writes there via the pipeline; the `_gate` harness writes to a temp dir.
- ⚠️ The bench rig is currently unsafe (damaged clamp, swivel collision risk) — no rig actuation without explicit safety clearance.

- **2026-08-26 (c) - THE FROZEN REGISTRIES ARE GONE: every target, template, CAT and boot fact is now resolved PER RUN from that run's own snapshot, and two profiles can compile at the same time without seeing each other. NOT COMMITTED (explicit instruction).** **THE DEFECT, AND IT WAS ORDER-DEPENDENT.** `TargetRegistry`, `TemplateManifest`, `TemplateMap` and `TargetBootstrap` were static classes that computed their derived sets on FIRST TOUCH. Whichever configuration reached them first decided what every later run in the process saw - so a second profile (another twin, a concurrent generation, a test with its own bundle) compiled its recipe against its own declarations and its device tree against the first run's. Both halves were individually valid, so nothing reported it. `TargetRegistry`'s cache was keyed on the declaration list's OBJECT IDENTITY, which means whether a run saw its own targets depended on whether YAML happened to hand back the same list instance. **All four are DELETED** (-495 lines) and replaced by `TargetIndex` + `TemplateIndex` (+537), built in the `CompilerConfiguration` constructor from the declarations that snapshot already holds - so the resolved view cannot outlive or contradict the declarations it came from. ~170 call sites now read `cfg.Targets` / `cfg.Manifest` / `ctx.Manifest`. **PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED:** six new `ProfileIsolationTests` (A->B->A in one process, 24 concurrent loads over two bundles, CAT selection, port spelling, an invalid bundle refused without disturbing a valid one) **ALL SIX FAIL when the frozen-on-first-use behaviour is temporarily reinstated, and pass when it is removed.** **PER-RUN PROFILE BUNDLES:** `CompilerConfiguration.Load(paths, profileRoot)` reads a run's OWN Config folder; the default bundle keeps its mtime cache, a named root is read fresh and shared with nothing. templates.yml is now validated against smc-rig.yml at the SNAPSHOT rather than inside templates.yml's own loader - which reached for `RigCatalog.Current` and so checked one run's templates against another run's roles. **STATIC READS IN THE SEMANTIC LAYER: ZERO.** Two new architecture rules, each proven to bite by injecting a breach: Domain/Planning may not name `TargetRegistry`/`TemplateManifest`/`TargetBootstrap`/`RingHost`/`ProcessPhaseTransport` or reach `*.Current`/`CompilerConfiguration.Default`; and `TargetIndex`/`TemplateIndex` may hold no static state at all. The configuration allowlist LOST its "registry" category - there are no registries left, so the exemption a future frozen static could have hidden behind is gone - and now also catches `CompilerConfiguration.Default`, the ONE named process-wide snapshot, which exists only for `ControllerMap` (the separately-owned HMI calls it statically) and `MapperConfig`'s forwarders. **A THIRD RULE, for a hazard nothing exploits yet:** the loaders hand every run the SAME cached DTO, so one `Add()` would reach every other run in the process. Nothing writes through the snapshot today; a rule now fails the build if anything starts, and `ProfileIsolationTests` asserts the sharing that makes the rule necessary. **THE TRANSACTION'S LAST HOLE CLOSED.** `ProjectTransaction` fell back to writing IN PLACE whenever a project root could not be derived - which conflates "no project here" (safe: nothing to protect) with "a project whose .dfbproj is missing, renamed or unreadable" (a tree that would be overwritten with no way back). The compiler now REFUSES it (`UnsatisfiableInterlockException`) naming actuator, state, both conditions, their settled numbers and the fix - put each `Bearing_PnP` condition in its OWN ConditionGroup, which VueOne reads as alternatives. A throw anywhere leaves the previous project byte-for-byte; staging is removed, and an abandoned one from a killed run is swept next time. Proven by test, not asserted. **A REQUIRED STAGE THAT FAILS ABORTS.** 19 stages are declared Required or Optional at the call site rather than inferred from how a message reads; topology, resource, sysres, HCF, registration and MQTT are Required. The `"FB1"`/`"FB2"` literals that told a boot FB from a component went with it (`TargetBootstrap.BootRoles`/`InitRole`). 9 tests pin every refusal. **A RING IS A SET OF TARGETS.** `ReportGraph` decided membership with `"feed"`, `"assembly"` and `"*"`; it now builds a `TargetPartition` (union-find) over the declared targets - targets hosting one station share a ring, a device that runs no process joins whatever drives it, a merge joins them all - and a discharge member is carried per COMPONENT onto its commander's ring. `Domain` returns a typed `ReportDomainId` whose identity is the connected component, so renaming a station cannot change which reporters share a state_table. 8 tests, including the same topology under unrelated names. **⚠️ ONE CONCESSION: `Domain(string)` survives beside `DomainId(string)` because `HmiSlotIndex` takes it as a `Func<string,string>` grouping key and HMI was out of scope.** **MQTT FOLLOWS THE RESOURCE.** `SystemLayoutInjector` pinned `feedSuffix = "M262"`, spelled `Telemetry_M580` and branched per controller; it now walks `telemetry.yml connections:` in declaration order - which is emission order - for every target `ctx.Emits`, taking name, client, where it is drawn (`drawnAt`) and what starts it (`broughtUpBy`) from the declaration. `SysresFbMirror.BucketFor` lost its `Telemetry_RevPi` special case (the declaration answers it), its dead `MqttFmt_`/`MqttPub_` bridge route and its dead `*_CoverRingGate` routes, and its Feed fallback is now the declared capability rather than `PlcAssignment.M262`. **NET-POSITIVE, OWNED:** +381/-151. The additions are the typed models and validation the task itself mandates - union-find + `ReportDomainId` (~42), bring-up validation (~31), the execution declaration and its loader (~65), the connection's drawn/started columns (~19). A dead-member sweep across non-HMI CodeGen finds nothing left to remove, so the residual is the cost of typing four facts that were previously four literals. **NEXT (user): nothing to deploy - core output is byte-identical by construction. `C:\Demonstrator` NOT written (newest file still 12 Aug).**

The plan is now a flattened SUM OF PRODUCTS: `InterlockRule` gained `TermCount`, a row with >=1 heads an alternative and the rows after it are its remaining terms, and `CommonInterlockEvaluator.Evaluate` gained a nested loop that blocks only when one whole alternative holds. Zero alternatives of one term is the plain rule, so a guard with no conjunction in it produces exactly what it always did - which is why Bearing_PnP's six rows are unchanged while Shaft_Hr's three became ONE three-term rule. **REAL RIG CONSEQUENCE:** those four actuators now block on ALL of their terms where they used to block on any one. Provably unsatisfiable = an inert safety rule. - **2026-08-20 (f) — CORE MADE AN INDUSTRIAL COMPILER: a guard now keeps its TRUTH, an interlock guards what it NAMES and is validity-checked at runtime, nothing truncates, every process is emitted on whichever target owns it, hardware behaviour sits behind one backend contract, and the gate is versioned in the repo. an operator presses a PAYLOAD** - CTCNF carries both RUN and STOP, MCNF carries Setup (real) and Initial Position (interlock-bypassing) - so a per-event verdict can only ever get one of each pair right. **THE MODEL IS NOW PER ACTION:** one small typed `capabilities.actions` table in hmi.yml names 15 actions by the exact call they make (`fires: MCNF(9)`) or the tag they write (`writes: toWork1`), each `provedBy` a capability, each optionally carrying its own artefact proof. **NO control, handler or symbol name is written down anywhere** - an action is located by its CALL, so a renamed button cannot escape the gate. **TWO PAYLOAD PROOFS, BOTH READ FROM THE DEPLOYED ECC:** `distinctFrom` fails when every transition guarded on the payload also accepts another (this is what refuses Manual mode - `FiveStateActuator.fbt:95,107` accept `mode = 1` and `mode = 2` in the same condition, so mode 2 changes nothing), and `interlockedBy` fails when any such transition omits the interlock term (this is what refuses Initial Position - `FiveStateActuator.fbt:110` is `ToWork -> ToHome [mode = 9]` with **no interlock term at all**, and `SevenStateCentreHomeActuator.fbt:140,143` the same). **NON-FIREABLE IS NOW STRUCTURAL, by the mechanism each action actually uses:** a FIRED action has its `FireEvent_X(payload)` call DELETED from the staged faceplate and its control disabled via `Enabled = false` - the reference's own idiom, taken from `Five_State_Actuator_CAT_sSetup.cnv.cs:28`; a TAG-BOUND action is UNBOUND (its `TagName` assignment removed), because deleting code-behind does nothing to a control NxtControl writes by itself; and where EVERY action on a symbol is withheld the monitoring variant is placed instead (ManualScreen now carries `Process1_Generic.sAutomatic`, not `sManual`). **RESULT, all four twins: the committed panel contains exactly TWO live command calls - `FireEvent_MCNF(1)` and `FireEvent_MCNF(3)`.** **THREE MORE DEFECTS FOUND AND FIXED EN ROUTE.** (1) **The mode-reach walk followed the REPORT RING.** `HmiModeReach` walked every adapter edge, so it wandered from Station1's actuators round the `stateRprtCmd` ring into another controller's and reported Setup as available on 13 instances - including the BX1 covers, whose `stationAdptr_in` **has no source at all** and whose `ActuatorCore.mode` therefore holds its `InitialValue=1` forever. - **2026-08-05 (b) — PROCESS-STEP MQTT TELEMETRY: three defects found on the wire and fixed. Every process published `{state:0}` forever; then it published the right number ~10x too often; and Assembly/Disassembly could not name most of their phases at all. All three fixed, 42/42 tests, 9 per-file commits PUSHED (HEAD==origin/main 8612c94). `C:\Demonstrator` NOT regenerated by me — the user must Generate + Build + Deploy.** **DEFECT 1 — MINE, the classic unassociated-OutputVar trap (the SAME one this file already documents for `CurrentStep`/`CurrentStepType`/`WaitSatisfied`).** I declared `CurrentProcessState`, assigned it in `LoadStep`/`AdvanceStep` and wired it to `MqttFmt.state`, but never WITH-associated it to any event. An OutputVar is only sampled onto its data connections when an associated event fires, so the value never left the FB and every subscriber read the initial 0 — 1,340 captured rows, 100% zero, while components beside them published fine. **DEFECT 2 — PUBLISH STORM, and it also explains the "flicker".** The engine emits `SCNF` from FOUR ECStates, one being `WAIT_STEP`, which is re-entered from `WAIT_HOLD` on **every `state_change`** (every ring message from any component); `check_wait` never touches `CurrentProcessState`, so it republished the same unchanged phase for as long as a process waited. Measured: **Feed 114 messages for 16 real phase changes; Assembly 618 for 58; Disassembly 608, with `Initialize` alone 214 times** (that is the user's "Disassembly publishes before it starts" — it was parked on its opening handshake shouting one phase). One minute of capture held **1,406 rows**. The apparent step-back to the previous phase is a consequence: hundreds of messages in flight at **QoS 1, which does not guarantee ordering**, so a straggler from the previous burst lands after the new phase. **FIX:** new EventOutput `PHASECNF` emitted ONLY from `IDLE1` — entered exactly once per recipe row (from INIT, from ADVANCE, and from WAIT_STEP once satisfied) — carrying `CurrentProcessState`; the publisher fans off that instead of SCNF. Adding an emission cannot alter control flow: no transition tests it, only the publisher consumes it. **DEFECT 3 — I PUBLISHED THE WRONG NUMBER. `State_Number` is a BROADCAST SLOT, not a state id** (this file already says so; I did not apply it). The twin fills it in only where a peer process watches: **14 of 32 Assembly states and 18 of 29 Disassembly states carry 0**, and Assembly REUSES 1-7 for two states each (`Clamping_Part`==`Cover_Pnp_advanced`==2). So `Unclamping`, the ejector and the robot were one indistinct 0, and only 4 of Assembly's 20 phases were readable. **FIX:** publish a DENSE per-process ordinal taken from the twin's own declaration order (stable across regenerations, independent of the chain walk; 0 reserved for "no owning state"), plus a new `ProcessPhaseMapEmitter` writing `process-phases.json` — the ordinal→name map — to the **PARENT of the EAE project root** (`C:\Demonstrator\process-phases.json`), deliberately OUTSIDE the solution because EAE enumerates its own tree and an unregistered file inside it becomes a Solution Integrity complaint. Nothing generated references it, so a missing/stale map only degrades a subscriber to bare numbers. **RESULT (decoded from the generated tree): 0 unnumbered rows on all three processes** — Feed 7 phases, Assembly 25 (incl. `Clamping_Part`), Disassembly 27 (incl. `Unclamping`, `EjectorForward`), every one mapped to a name. **LOGGER:** `MQTT/mqtt_to_sqlite.py` reads the map and stores/prints the name — live output now reads `smc/process/Disassembly {state:29}   Unclamping`. Resolved at CAPTURE time, not on read, so a later regeneration cannot silently relabel history; `phase` column added additively (older rows keep NULL — they predate the map); degrades to numbers with no map; `SMC_PHASE_MAP` overrides the location. **VERIFIED:** generated-tree checks all PASS (PHASECNF on IDLE1 only, absent from WAIT_STEP, exactly one emitter, publisher not on SCNF, map covers 9/32/29 phases, zero 0-rows, recipes still present); live end-to-end through the real broker and the real `run()` entry point (Unclamping/Clamping_Part/FeederReturning stored + printed, component rows correctly have no phase); **42/42 tests**. **⚠️ TEST SCOPE, stated honestly:** only `Feed_Station` is compiled offline — `Assembly_Station`/`Disassembly` reach across stations for their state_table slots and throw `'Bearing_Gripper' has no state_table slot on this ring` when compiled in isolation (which is why the original author only ever tested Feed). Their ordinals are verified against the generated tree instead; what they contribute offline is a guard pinning that their `State_Number` IS ambiguous, so a future "simplification" back to publishing it fails with the reason attached. **⚠️ I POLLUTED THE LIVE CAPTURE AND CLEANED IT:** my `mosquitto_pub` test messages reached the user's running logger; 10 injected rows removed from `smc.db` + `smc.jsonl` (backups `*.bak_before_prune`). Also noted 105 duplicate `(ts,topic,state)` groups in 5,555 rows — that is QoS 1 at-least-once redelivery, expected, not a logger fault. **NEXT (user): Generate from `_se` → EAE Build → Deploy → the log should show ~7/25/27 phases per cycle by NAME instead of ~1,400 rows a minute of `{state:0}`. The 1,340 bad rows are still in the DB; `RUN_COMMANDS.txt` documents a time-bounded DELETE that drops them without losing the component history.**

- **2026-08-05 — REVPI FINISHED AND PROVEN: the deploy target was pointing at the RevPi HOST instead of its CONTAINER, and the Modbus broker was an ORPHAN instance. Both root-caused, fixed, and proven by an executed generation (56/56 artefact checks + 29 new in-repo tests). CodeGen 0 err 0 warn; MapperUI 0 err; NOT committed at write time.** **ROOT CAUSE #1 — ADDRESSING, and it is a two-part category error.** `Config/device.yml` set `revPi.targetIp = .1`. Commit `cc8d07f` had changed it `.6 -> .1` reasoning *"the Softdpac runtime answers on 192.168.1.1 (ping-verified), not Jyotsna-reference .6 (times out)"*. **`.6` timed out because the container had never been created** (an unprovisioned endpoint, not a wrong address), and **`.1` answered ping because `.1` is the address the Mapper ITSELF emits for the HMI panel's Soft dPAC container** (`Template Library/HMI/Deployment/Equipment_HMIP6_1.json:32`). So EAE's deploy target was aimed at a HOST, which never listens on 51443 — Deploy/Login could not succeed no matter how healthy the RevPi was. **`.6` IS COMPILER-PROVEN:** EAE resolved `Revolution_Pi.RES0` to `nxtv3://192.168.1.6:51300` in the reference solution's own `CrossComm/…commdesc.xml`. The host/container roles are settled by the rule the known-good BX1 obeys — the endpoint carrying `SoftdpacManagerDEO` (8080) at the equipment ROOT is the host (BX1 `.209`), the nested `SoftdpacContainer` carrying a `RuntimeDEO` with a real `logicalDeviceId` is the container (BX1 `.151`, runtime open). Final: **host `.1`, container `.6`**; `.1` was freed by moving the HMI panel's container to `.3` — safe because BOTH its RuntimeDEOs bind the null device `00000000-…` (verified in the live generated tree, lines 62 and 151), i.e. it executes nothing; the real Harmony HMI is `HMI_NET` (`a441cfb6…`) bound to `Workstation_1` at `.2`, untouched. **⚠ `hostIp .1` IS PROVISIONAL — no project file prescribes a RevPi host address (the reference says `.2`, which is the Harmony host here and is 3-way claimed there). Confirm `.1` against the MAC printed on the physical RevPi (KUNBUS OUI) before deploying. That is the ONE check that cannot be made offline.** **ROOT CAUSE #2 — `RevPI_IO` WAS AN ORPHAN INSTANCE, two symptoms one cause.** (a) `RevPiIoBrokerInjector` searched only `<FBNetwork>` while `SyslayBuilder` emits `<SubAppNetwork>`, so the syslay half of the injector was **unreachable dead code** (generated syslay: 0 `RevPI_IO` vs 2 `BX1_IO`); (b) it never stamped the `Mapping` attribute that `Bx1IoBrokerInjector.cs:545` stamps — `RevPI_IO` was the ONLY non-boot FB in the whole tree lacking one. A resource FB with no `Mapping` has no application instance behind it (EAE's "Repair Instances" class). Fixed by mirroring BX1 exactly (the shipped `BX1_IO` carries `ID == Mapping == F6C04A4BA6FA8593`). **ALSO FIXED:** the RevPi `.sysres` was registered `<None SystemDevice>` because `DfbprojRegistrar` hardcoded the BX1 sysdev id — a Soft dPAC's sysres must be `<Compile SystemResource>` or **EAE never compiles its HWConfig**, which for the RevPi is its Modbus MASTER (this was the prime suspect for "deploys but no Modbus traffic"); the parity validator only enumerated the RevPi when `FeedStationController == RevPi`, a mode nothing can select, so **the one reachable RevPi mode had ZERO validation**; and the sweep left an orphan indentation node per removed FB so the resource grew on every re-deploy. **NEW FAIL-LOUD GUARDS:** `RevPiSelectionValidator` (pre-generation) rejects the whole-Feed swap — `PLC_RW_REVPI` exposes IO for exactly {Feeder, Checker, PartInHopper}, so a full swap would strand Transfer/Ejector/Robot/PartAtAssembly with no physical IO — plus the contradictory full+partial combination and any component the coupler cannot serve; `TopologyAddressValidator` (post-generation) fails on host==container, malformed addresses, and any **Soft dPAC CONTAINER** address collision. Container collisions are FATAL (a container is a macvlan child with its own MAC and is EAE's deploy target); duplicated HOST NICs only WARN, because the shipped tree AND the reference both carry `.2` twice and both import into EAE — failing there would reject known-good output. **THE SWAPPABLE SET IS DERIVED, NOT HARDCODED:** `RevPiIoBrokerInjector.CoveredComponents` is computed from the coupler's own Sensors/Coils tables, and the UI restriction + validator + tooltip all read it, so extending `PLC_RW_REVPI` automatically extends what may be selected. **PROVEN, not asserted:** a throwaway harness (`scratchpad/revpi_prove`, the `C:\_gate\manifestdump` precedent) runs the REAL emitters over a copy of `C:\Demonstrator` — **56/56** checks: macvlan type 0 + parent `NIC_2\eth0`, ARM image `v24.1.25090.08`, Manager 8080, RuntimeDEO→sysdev, container on DeviceNetwork_1, no stale `/16`, every referenced domain declared, sysres non-empty hosting Feeder/Checker/PartInHopper/`RevPI_IO` **with Mapping**, `RevPI_IO` in the syslay, `<Compile SystemResource>`, topologyproj + Folders.xml, Modbus `.hcf` LinkNames resolve, Feeder/Checker gone from M262, **no dangling `.hcf` binding**, no fatal address collision, content-idempotent. **REGRESSION SCOPE IS BOUNDED AND MEASURED:** in default M262 mode the ONLY topology file that changes vs the base tree is `Equipment_HMIP6_1.json` (the inert panel's address) — proven by a control run in the same harness. Plus **29 xunit tests** in `MapperTests` (which had ZERO test files); the user's tracked `MapperTests.csproj` change was preserved untouched. **⚠️ CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER DOCS (both were wrong and are now annotated):** `REVPI_DEPLOYMENT_FIX_PLAN.md` claimed `.1` was correct and that "the Mapper already fully supports RevPi" — both refuted above. And **`MapperConfig.RevPiBridgeInsideComposite` DOES NOT EXIST in any `.cs`** (repo-wide grep is empty; the 2026-07-12 entry below cites it and promises a "flip to FALSE" revert — that lever was never implemented, RevPi internalization is unconditional, and the external-bridge fallback emits no publisher/subscriber/scan FBs). **⚠️ THE GATE CANNOT EXERCISE REVPI:** `C:\_gate\gate.sh` sets `CONTROLLERS="M262"` and its hidden runner has zero RevPi references, so a RevPi gate needs three coordinated changes (a controller flag on the runner, RevPi emitter calls in its `Generate()`, and passing `$ctrl`); the runner's `Config/` is also stale in `smc-rig.yml` AND `device.yml`. **FULL SPEC:** `Docs/REVPI_PROVISIONING.md` — terminology (EAE deploys a compiled IEC 61499 boot project; Soft dPAC executes the SAME runtime, `IEC61499.dll` md5-identical across M262/M580/Soft_dPAC, packaged as image `softdpac`; **zero** UAO artifacts installed, so choosing RevPi is a HOSTING decision, not a runtime-technology one), the macvlan proof (`VLanType.MacVLan=0` reflected from `Topology.dll` 1.0.25093.1), the address table, and the 14-stage deployment contract. **NEXT (user): `arp -a` to confirm `.1` is the RevPi (KUNBUS OUI) → set Feeder/Checker Device = RevPi → Generate → EAE Build → Manage Soft dPAC → Apply (creates the macvlan + container; run EAE elevated) → `.6:51443` opens → Deploy+Login with outputs inhibited.**

- **2026-07-30 (d) — THE PLACE IS A 178 mm DROP, NOT A PLACE. Extracted the taught poses; that is why the part lands askew and the feeder pushes it diagonally. The VC-side fix is a re-teach the gateway cannot do; the gateway's own contribution is fixed. Tests 104/104 + 10/10 + 5/5. Committed per-file + PUSHED (2114640). EJECTOR NOT TOUCHED (user directive).** **THE GEOMETRY, read out of the taught program:** `Partpick` grips at **P5 z = 45.86 mm** with the tool tilted **20°** off vertical, and approaches/retracts through P4 at z = 82.35 — i.e. it is a proper pick: approach, grip, lift. `Partplace` is **one linear move to P2 at z = 223.72 mm, tool straight down (0° tilt), then the gripper opens.** There is NO descent and NO retract. So the part is let go **178 mm above the height it was picked from**, and its orientation differs by **20°** from how it was gripped. It falls, lands rotated, and the feeder then pushes it diagonally — exactly what the user sees. **THE FIX BELONGS IN THE VC PROGRAM and is a re-teach, not a gateway change:** Partplace needs Partpick's shape — approach above the nest (P2 is a fine approach), **DESCEND to the nest surface, release there, retract** — and the release orientation should match the orientation the part was gripped at unless the nest genuinely wants it rotated. I have NOT guessed those targets: a wrong z or tool angle drives the arm into the fixture, and the correct pose takes two minutes to jog and touch up in VC's own OLP editor where it can be seen. **WHAT WAS MINE, AND IS FIXED:** the `released` rule completed a place the instant the part detached, which cancelled the taught trailing `Delay` via the next chain step's clearCallStack — so the gripper withdrew while the part was still dropping. That rule was correct when the trailing dwell was an unscalable 1.6 s of pure lateness; **`dwellFactor` superseded it** — the dwell now runs at the rig's own rate, so it IS the rig's settle time. The early cut is removed and the routine runs out. **IT COSTS NOTHING: the release still lands at 5.016 s (rig 5.012), because the settle comes after it** — only the step's completion moves, and the chain is 6.206 s against the rig's 7.827, so there is 1.6 s of slack. **NEXT (user): re-paste `Tools/statesync/vc_gateway.py` into `0 #2` (nothing to restart — statesync already carries 2.0/2.0 from epoch `2026-07-30T09:47:11Z`). Expect `routine_motion_scaled ... delays=2` and Partplace no longer completing `via=released`. The part will still land from 178 mm until Partplace is re-taught with a descent — that is the remaining fix, and it is in the VC program.**

- **2026-07-30 (c) — MY DWELL FIX SILENTLY DID NOTHING: a baseline recorded by the previous build was reused whole, so the taught Delay was never written. Fixed. Ejector NOT touched (user directive). Scaled it would have been 3333. **ROOT CAUSE, MINE:** `scaleRoutineMotion` stores the taught baseline in `AppliedScale` and only re-captures when the row COUNT changes. The rows written by the previous build contain the six linear speed/accel properties but no `Delay`, because that build did not collect it. Same count, so the staleness check passed, the incomplete rows were reused, and the dwell was never written — while the log still reported the routine as retimed. **THE FIX — MERGE, DO NOT REPLACE.** A property already in the baseline is kept (it is genuinely taught, and re-reading it would read our own scaled output); a property MISSING from the baseline has by definition never been written by us, so the model still holds its taught value and it is captured now. The "already at this rate" short-circuit also had to learn about this: it now writes whenever the baseline just GREW, otherwise the newly captured Delay would sit taught forever behind a `factor unchanged` guard. A row-count mismatch still forces a full reset and logs `routine_baseline_reset`. **THE PART STUCK AGAIN so the robot could not be judged** — it ran the whole chain `carrying ['2F-85']` with no Part, Partpick completing via `idle_after_start` and Partplace falling back to `motion_settled` (the release rule correctly stayed silent with nothing to release). Expect Partpick ~3333 ms and the release ~5017 ms into the task.**

A test must not depend on a log someone can truncate. `rig_cadence.py` is running again, so the next rig run recaptures a full trace. **NEXT (user, two actions): (1) in VC re-paste `Tools/statesync/vc_gateway.py` into `0 #2` (the release rule lives there); (2) nothing to do for statesync — I already restarted it, epoch `2026-07-29T14:09:36Z`, so the 200/340 ms ejector is live. Then run one cycle: the ejector should fire ~3x quicker and the robot should drop the part ~0.5 s earlier.**

- **2026-07-27 — FOUR LIVE-SYNC DEFECTS ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED. VC 60/60 + replay 10/10; VueOne 25/25 + models 5/5. Both VueOne projects build 0 err; binaries + config deployed to `Published_Alper`. Committed per-file + PUSHED.** **(1) UR3e "227 s timeout" was NEVER a stuck routine — the SIMULATION WAS STOPPED.** `gateway_down active=2` at 14:18:27, no ticks for 3m46s, then on the first tick back BOTH the UR3e (227794 ms) and an innocent Pusher stroke (227029 ms vs 1125 ms commanded) "completed". Elapsed was measured on `datetime.now()` while the loop ticks in SIMULATION time. FIX: `simNowMs()` reads `getSimulation().SimTime` (tick-accumulator fallback), and `OnStop`/`OnReset` now explicitly FAIL every in-flight item instead of leaking it. Also makes motion correct at non-realtime SimSpeed. **(2) UR3e Home `never_started` after 755 ms** was a 750 ms heuristic firing on a routine that is simply indistinguishable from one that finished between 20 ms polls — and it FLUSHED the lane, stranding Home behind Partplace forever. FIX: grace 3000 ms and the unknown case reports `completed / via=unobserved / verified=false / observed=false` and RELEASES the lane. Only a proven verify mismatch or a real timeout fails now. **(3) Ejector forward stroke.** The model exonerates itself — byte-identical to the Pusher (same servo, same stock `ServoController_Script`, `_Open=0/_Closed=90`, joint limit 90). The defect was the CONTRACT: a signal command completed because `durationMs` elapsed, never because the joint arrived. FIX: signal completion is now ENDPOINT evidence (`|joint-target| <= max(0.5, 1% stroke)`), with an EDGE GUARANTEE — a VC boolean signal fires `OnSignal` only on a CHANGE, so re-signalling the value it already holds moves nothing (forward dead, return fine); the gateway forces the edge, but only when the joint is provably not at target. No motion by the deadline now publishes `signal_no_motion` / `target_not_reached` with jointBefore/jointAfter/endpointError/OpenState/ClosedState. **(4) VueOne stuck at Bearing_PnP — TWO causes, both generic.** (a) `VcMqttClient` subscribed `prefix + "/#"` and excluded only `_bridge`, so `vc/command` AND `vc/event` were parsed as rig observations — both carry `vcId`+`stateName`, so every state was applied up to 3x and the event re-applied a state the twin had left. Bearing_PnP is hit hardest because it emits the most commands+events. (b) `shadow-config` carried the EAE CAT runtime vocabulary, not the TWIN's names: the twin calls state 1 `TurningPick` (not `ToWork1`) and the home leg `TurningHome` (not `ToHome`), so VueOne's by-name resolution silently dropped them. FIX (a): canonical observation filter `<prefix>/+/+/state`, configurable via `StateSync.TopicFilter`, plus a belt-and-braces `IsObservationTopic` guard. FIX (b): data-only — `bearing_pnp` 1->`TurningPick`, 5->`TurningHome`; `robot` 1->`Partpick`; `clamp` 0->`Home Pos`. The `_se`/`_vc`/`_` models now resolve EVERY publishable state, 0 unmatched. **ALSO in the VueOne layer, all model-agnostic:** full envelope kept (`seq/epoch/ts/state/quality/sourceTopic/station/component/present`) in `UnsStateMessage`; new `UnsOrderingGate` (public, pure, testable) does epoch reset + retained bootstrap + duplicate + out-of-order rejection; new `VcMqttStats` counters; MQTT drain DECOUPLED from `VOLogicEngine.CycleTick` via a 50 ms bounded-batch WinForms pump with a re-entrancy guard, so a stopped local engine no longer freezes rig following; `StateSync.RigFollowMode` gates the `mfSimulator==null` early-returns (OFF = offline simulation byte-for-byte as before); vcId resolution now reports 0-match and AMBIGUOUS(>1) instead of silently returning; unmatched stateName logged; client id suffixed with the process id so two VueOne instances stop evicting each other. **⚠ FINDING: no Control.xml under `Published_Alper\workspace\system\` declares ANY VcID (0 of 24) — those models cannot be followed at all. The followable family is `OneDrive\Documents\VueOne\system\SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes*`.** **NEXT (user, one action): in VC paste `Tools/statesync/vc_gateway.py` into `0 #2`, Reset -> Play, start VueOne from `Published_Alper`, CONNECT TO RIG, run one cycle, then `python tests/check_live.py`.**

The manifest therefore stores `Role` and `MirrorToSysres` as SEPARATE columns; deriving either from the other silently changes membership. **27 lists were deliberately KEPT SEPARATE with a stated reason**, the important ones being: `ComponentTypeToCat` (a rival CAT selector keyed on `{Type}_{StateCount}`, case-INSENSITIVE, distinct from `TemplateMap.ResolveActuatorCatType`); the BX1/RevPi/Telemetry deploy blocks (config-gated deploy-then-patch units where position is load-bearing); `SweepRetiredType` (a name there means the OPPOSITE of a deploy-list name) — its position BETWEEN the Basic and Adapter loops is preserved; `SystemLayoutInjector.UniversalCatTypes` (a Clean WIPE policy list, not an emission list); `NoRingAdapterTypes` (empty, but its comment records why it must stay distinct from `NoStationAdapterTypes`); and every component-INSTANCE-name and generated-FB-INSTANCE-name list, which live on a different axis entirely. `TemplateMap.NoStationAdapterCatTypes` was ALREADY correctly de-duplicated (ResourceWireEmitter holds a direct alias) so it needed no work. **VERIFICATION — the gate alone was NOT enough.** Two absorbed consumers (the CAT dependency edges, reached only from MainForm's secondary deploy buttons, and the port contract, a read-only startup diagnostic) drive paths the gate never walks, so a membership error there would be invisible. My first check was a regex parser over the C# and it reported 7 false failures — useless. Replaced with `C:\_gate\manifestdump` + `verify_manifest.sh`, which loads the REAL compiled `CodeGen.dll`, dumps all 30 derived sets and diffs them against a recorded baseline: **every set reproduces exactly, including the six deploy inventories in their original ORDER and all 8 CAT dependency edges.** **ONE DEAD FILTER DELETED:** `ResourceWireEmitter` carried `.Where(s => true || !IsTopCoverSensor(s))` — the `true ||` short-circuits, so the cover-sensor opt-out never excluded anything. Restored to six, which is also what the column's own definition requires — `FiveStateActuator` IS a patch target (`EnsureFiveStateInputPoll` 07-25(n), the `homeSensorFitted` InputVar 07-26(b)), so a copy-if-absent extraction would keep a stale shape. **⚠️ ALSO CORRECTED FROM THE ADVERSARIAL PASS:** my `Robot_Task_CAT` row comment claimed the pair deploys "on their own gate (`EnableRobotTaskTail`)" — that gate was removed 2026-06-17 and the comment was six weeks stale. - **2026-07-26 (m) — MODEL-FILE INCIDENT, FOUND AND REPAIRED: `_vc/Control.xml` was OVERWRITTEN with the `_sw5_noclamp` content (five-state swivel), destroying the real 13-state `_vc`. Restored. No Mapper code changed.** **WHAT HAPPENED:** `_sw5_noclamp` was created at 16:41:46 by copying `_vc` and swapping in `_sw5`'s five-state `Bearing_PnP`; at **16:46:42** `_vc\Control.xml` was written with that SAME content (193596 chars, byte-identical to `_sw5_noclamp`, 31 components, no Clamp, swivel `AtWork1/ToWork2/AtWork2/ToWork1/AtWork1Finished`). **MECHANISM — CORRECTED, and it was NOT a spontaneous VueOne re-save (my first account was wrong):** a VueOne model's identity lives in the `<System>` header of its OWN `Control.xml` (`<SystemID>` + `<Name>`), and `MetaData.json` merely mirrors it. `_sw5_noclamp` was built by copying `_vc`'s `Control.xml`, and only the new `MetaData.json` got a fresh uuid/name — the copied `Control.xml` header still declared `SystemID SYS-d4d52dc3…` and `Name SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes_vc`. **VueOne keys the loaded model on the Control.xml header, not the folder**, so it displayed `_vc` in the title bar while showing `_sw5_noclamp`'s five-state swivel, and on save it wrote back to `_vc`'s folder. A duplicate `SystemID` is therefore a *destructive* condition, not a cosmetic one. **FIXED:** `_sw5_noclamp/Control.xml` header now carries its own `SYS-e3d65e90-…` + `SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes_sw5_noclamp` (backup `Control.before_identity_fix_20260726.xml`); a sweep of all 10 models under `…\VueOne\system` confirms every `Control.xml` header now agrees with its `MetaData.json` and its folder name. **⚠️ ONE PRE-EXISTING COLLISION LEFT, NOT TOUCHED (not mine, and it is a live model): `SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes_` (trailing underscore — with Clamp, 13-state, last edited 2026-07-21) ALSO declares `SystemID SYS-d4d52dc3…`, i.e. the same id as `_vc`. Loading one and saving can write into the other. Give one of them a fresh uuid before using either.** **RULE: when cloning a VueOne model, ALWAYS rewrite the `<SystemID>` and `<Name>` in the new `Control.xml` header — copying the folder alone produces two models that overwrite each other.** The generation the user then ran (`C:\Demonstrator`, 16:46:45, three seconds later) therefore produced **`_sw5_noclamp` logic under the `_vc` name** — which is exactly the tree that was safety-checked and passed, so that verdict still stands for that content; only the on-disk model named `_vc` was wrong. **THE RESTORE:** `Control.before_handshake_statenumber_20260726.xml` (203013 chars, the true 13-state `_vc`) + re-applying the one approved edit `Disassembly/handshake State_Number 0 -> 11` (11 is free; 0-10 are taken) = 203014 chars, exactly the size the post-fix `_vc` had before the clobber. **Both the restored file AND the clobbered one are kept** — `Control.restored_13state_20260726.xml` (insurance against a second VueOne re-save) and `Control.CLOBBERED_by_sw5noclamp_20260726.xml`. **VERIFIED with a real XML parser (not a regex):** `_vc` = 31 components, no Clamp, 13-state swivel, `handshake`=11; `_sw5_noclamp` = 31 components, no Clamp, 5-state swivel, `handshake`=11; `_sw5`/`_se` = 32 components, with Clamp, untouched. `_sw5_noclamp` MetaData `Description` cleared to `""` (it was overrunning the model name in VueOne's picker); every model now carries an empty Description and its own `SYS-` uuid. **THE TWO MODELS ARE DELIBERATELY DIFFERENT AND MUST STAY SO: `_vc` = no clamp + THIRTEEN-state Bearing_PnP; `_sw5_noclamp` = no clamp + FIVE-state Bearing_PnP.** **⚠️ OPERATING RULE, RE-LEARNED THE HARD WAY (third occurrence): VueOne re-saves the loaded model's Control.xml from memory at unpredictable moments, so an external edit to a model that is OPEN can be silently reverted — or, as here, a DIFFERENT model's content can be written into it. NEVER edit a Control.xml while `vueOneSystem.exe` is running, and after any external edit RELOAD the model in VueOne before generating.** **NEXT (user): close VueOne WITHOUT saving `_vc`, reopen it, and confirm Bearing_PnP shows the 13 states; if it shows 5 again, VueOne re-clobbered on close — copy `Control.restored_13state_20260726.xml` over `Control.xml` with VueOne shut.**

ALL THREE models generate; CONTROL ARTIFACTS BYTE-IDENTICAL (0 non-HMI diffs, se/vc/sw5). NOT committed, NOT deployed.** **WHAT IT DOES:** after the syslay is written, `HmiGenerator.Emit` reads it back and emits the whole EAE HMI project — screens (`.cnv.cs` + `.cnv.Designer.cs` + `.cnv.resx`), `CanvasesResolutionList.xml` (resolution 1024x768, StartCanvasClass, FirstCanvas, canvas topology), `HMI.csproj` item groups, and each `<CAT>.cfg`. **THE ONE BINDING FACT:** a faceplate binds to a component ONLY by `.TagName`, and `TagName` == the syslay `<FB ID>` the Mapper already generates (`FBIdGenerator`) — verified against the reference (`Feeder`=`E0AEF2679BD52F88` in both). So screens are derivable with no new control data. **FULLY MODEL-DRIVEN, NO COMPONENT LISTS, NO NAME CHECKS:** a component is drawable iff its emitted CAT declares an HMI interface (`IEC61499/<Type>/<Type>_HMI.fbt` exists) AND a faceplate template exists for that `Type`; infrastructure FBs (PLC_RW_BX1, SYMLINKMULTIVAR*, Telemetry, CaSAdptrTerminator, E_DELAY, Area/Station composites) declare no `_HMI.fbt` and are skipped with no exclusion list. Layout is shelf-packing driven by each template's declared `SymbolSize`, so a new/renamed/removed component just re-packs. **TEMPLATES (`Template Library/HMI/`):** `Shell/` = project-constant files copied verbatim (AssemblyInfo, GraphicsList, Alarms, Colors incl. the 134KB/63KB theme-merged `.cs`, Configurations, ImageStorage, Languages, Theme, `StartCanvas_2.*`, the `HMI.csproj` skeleton); `Faceplates/<CAT>/` = 7 types. `Area_CAT` and `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` come from Jyotsna's reference **because their `_HMI.fbt` is BYTE-IDENTICAL to what the Mapper emits** — so the rig gains her rich `sDefault`/`sSetup`/`sFault`/`sInterlock` faceplates unchanged. using it would need ProcessRuntime/CAT changes, which are out of scope — so **the manual step-through screen is NOT generated and is reported as unavailable-by-contract** (see `Docs/EAE_HMI_Generation_And_Harmony_Deployment_Investigation.md`). **SUPERSEDED SCAFFOLDING DELETED (net −44/+8 on existing files):** `TemplateArtifactDeployer.GenerateCfgFiles` (hardcoded a single `sDefault`, copy-if-absent — so the shipped `_setup` canvas was orphaned in every deployment) is GONE, replaced by the catalog-driven `HmiCatCfgEmitter.EmitAll` called from the same 2 sites; 16/16/17 canvases; 37/35/38 TagName bindings all resolving to real syslay FB Ids; 147/147/150 csproj items all existing, 0 duplicates, 0 dangling; 9 `.cfg` all resolving; canvas list == disk; StartCanvas + FirstCanvas resolve; - **2026-07-26 (h) — ⚠️ COLLISION ON THE RIG (`_vc`): Bearing_PnP moved carrying a bearing while Disassembly still held it. ROOT CAUSE: A PHASE WAS ANNOUNCED BEFORE THE WORK THAT MAKES IT TRUE. Fixed by emitting a state's owned work BEFORE its announcement. NOT committed/deployed.** **THE MECHANISM (read off the generated `_vc` recipe, not inferred):** Disassembly's state `bearing_pnp_home_pos` carries State_Number **0** and is the token Feed waits on (`Feed_Station/TransferAdvancing` references it). The compiler announced a non-entry phase BEFORE the state's rows, so the recipe read `… CMD bearing_pnp=1 · WAIT ==2 · CMD bearing_gripper=3 · WAIT ==0 · **CMD Disassembly=0** · CMD bearing_pnp=5 · WAIT ==0 · CMD transfer=3 …` — i.e. **it published "Disassembly reached bearing-home" while the arm was still at Work1 holding nothing yet homed, and before the Transfer returned.** Feed's `WAIT [18](Disassembly)==0` released there, Feed re-advanced the Transfer, Assembly's `WAIT transfer==2` opened, and Assembly began commanding `bearing_pnp` while Disassembly was still driving the same swivel. **THE INTERLOCKS CANNOT CATCH THIS AND ARE NOT AT FAULT:** `Bearing_PnP` carries 6 rules, all guarding the Work1<->Work2 CROSSING (`block 2->4` and `block 4->2` while `shaft_hr==2` / `coverpnp_hr==2` / `transfer==0`). Nothing blocks `0->2` out of home — the `(0,2,…)`/`(0,4,…)` home-departure gate was deliberately REVERTED on 2026-07-06 because it deadlocked the swivel against its own recipe. So two processes commanding the same swivel is a SEQUENCING failure the actuator layer is not designed to arbitrate. **THE FIX (ordering only, no new rows):** a phase announcement is a claim about the plant, so it is now emitted AFTER the state's owned work and before the conditions it then waits on — `Work() ; Announcement() ; conditions` for an ordinary phase, and `conditions ; Work() ; Announcement()` for the entry phase (a process still has not begun a cycle until authorised). The old rule ("announce before the conditions") was written when a state's rows came ENTIRELY from its outgoing conditions, which are the NEXT phase's preconditions, not this phase's work; once command ownership gave a state real work of its own (07-26(f)), announcing first became a lie. **RESULT:** `… CMD bearing_pnp=5 · WAIT [8](bearing_pnp)==0 · CMD Disassembly=0 · CMD transfer=3 · WAIT [6](transfer)==0 …` — Feed cannot be released until the swivel is home AND, one row later, the Transfer has returned, so Assembly cannot re-enter the swivel while Disassembly holds it. **VERIFIED on all three models:** recipe row counts IDENTICAL to before the fix (`_se` 17/58/63, `_sw5` 17/57/61, `_vc` 20/54/63) so nothing was added or dropped — only moved; every remaining "announce then CMD" adjacency is an announcement followed by the NEXT phase's work (checked by hand for each); CMD->WAIT slot agreement still 0 mismatches; HCF + parity + scanner PASS; two runs byte-identical. **⚠️ HONEST SEQUENCE OF EVENTS, so this is not misread:** 07-26(f) and (g) unblocked `_vc` (the Transfer never delivered, then the cover gripper never gripped), which meant the line reached the bearing/discharge tail for the FIRST time and exposed this pre-existing announcement-ordering defect. The defect was present before those fixes; it was simply unreachable. **⚠️ `_vc` MODEL HAZARD, reported not silently handled:** its Disassembly numbers 18 of its states State_Number **0** and reuses **6** for both `Initialize` and `TransferReturning`, so the ring token is heavily overloaded. The phase protocol's entry-arming (`WAIT ==6` then `WAIT ==0`) is what keeps it separable; a further collision in that model should be suspected here first.
