- **2026-07-26 (f) — `_vc` TRANSFER FIXED AT ITS SOURCE: COMMAND OWNERSHIP NOW COMES FROM THE ACTUATOR, NOT FROM WHOEVER WATCHES IT. CodeGen + MapperUI 0 err; DLL sha256 29B53338 -> all 4 VueOne integration locations; all three models generate `[Hcf][Validate] PASS` + `[Parity] PASS` + `[BX1][Scanner] OK`, 0 errors; two runs byte-identical (427 files, 0 differing); `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **ROOT CAUSE (user-diagnosed, confirmed from Control.xml):** `ProcessCompiler.EmitCondition` treated EVERY actuator condition on a process transition as an instruction to move that actuator. That is semantically backwards. A condition on a PROCESS transition (`Transfer/Advanced`) is an OBSERVATION; command ownership is declared on the ACTUATOR's own transition, whose `Sequence_Condition` names the `Process/State` that drives it. Reading observations as instructions made every process that merely watches an actuator also command it, and made one "has it returned yet" condition expand into a whole advance-and-return stroke (the trajectory walk crossed both stops). **WHAT THE MODEL ACTUALLY DECLARES:** all three models — `Transfer ReturnedHome->Advancing` owner `Feed_Station/TransferAdvancing`; `_se`/`_sw5` — `Advanced->Returning` owner `Feed_Station/TransferReturning`; **`_vc` — `Advanced->Returning` owner `Disassembly/TransferReturning`.** `_vc` deliberately holds the part on the Transfer across stations, so the upstream process advances it and the downstream one returns it. **THE FIX (net +150 lines, one compiler, no second path):** new `BuildOwnership` indexes every actuator transition gated by a `Process/State` under that process state; `EmitOwnedMoves` emits a state's owned movements first (chained where a state owns several legs of one actuator, e.g. the Checker's down-then-up stroke, following the chain from where the recipe last left it); `EmitCondition`'s command paths are DELETED and replaced by observation only. `EmitTrajectory` became the shared `DriveTo`, so the existing ActuatorGraph, command protocol, Row and serialization are reused verbatim; the graph gained `FirstStopVia` because a transition destination is normally a MOTION state and the stop actually commanded is the first stop reached through it. Ambiguity FAILS generation naming process, state, actuator and transitions — two legs leaving the SAME origin is a fork the model does not resolve. **ONE DOWNSTREAM GUARD HAD TO FOLLOW:** `SystemLayoutInjector`'s stranded-actuator check was PER-PROCESS ("advanced here must return here"), which is exactly what `_vc` legitimately violates. It now asks the model instead, via `ProcessCompiler.ProcessesCommandingHome` — stranded means NO process state anywhere owns a movement driving it home. Still a real guard, just correctly scoped. **BEFORE -> AFTER, `_vc`:** Feed `WAIT Disassembly==6 · WAIT Disassembly==0 · CMD transfer=1 · WAIT[6]==2 · CMD transfer=3 · WAIT[6]==0` (the Disassembly waits sat BEFORE the advance, so the part was never delivered) -> `CMD transfer=1 · WAIT[6]==2 · WAIT Disassembly==6 · WAIT Disassembly==0 · WAIT[6]==0`, **no `CMD transfer=3` from Feed**; Assembly `CMD transfer=1 · WAIT[6]==2` -> `WAIT[6]==2` only, **ZERO transfer commands**; Disassembly startup `CMD transfer=1 · WAIT[6]==2` -> `WAIT[6]==2` only, and its `TransferReturning` keeps exactly one `CMD transfer=3 · WAIT[6]==0`. **`_se`/`_sw5` Transfer rows are UNCHANGED** (Feed owns exactly one `CMD transfer=1` and one `CMD transfer=3`; Assembly and Disassembly own none). **SCOPE PROOF:** across all three models the ONLY differing files are the syslay + the recipe-carrying sysres; normalising the recipe parameters away makes every sysres IDENTICAL and leaves the syslay differing by 3 lines that are the embedded generation-report comment (row count + one new warning). Every `.hcf`, every CAT, every device/topology artefact byte-identical. **VERIFIED:** every WAIT source reachable on its consuming ring (`_vc` 0 off-ring; `_se`/`_sw5` Feed->slot 18 is the pre-existing CycleReady transport, IDENTICAL before and after); exactly one owning process per Transfer direction (`_vc` advance=Feed_Station, return=Disassembly; `_se`/`_sw5` both Feed_Station); HCF + parity + MQTT (3 Telemetry, ConnectionID=SMC) + cover-safe-start + BX1 scanner all PASS; determinism 427 files 0 differing. **⚠️ ONE INTENDED SIDE EFFECT, stated not hidden:** `_se`/`_sw5` Assembly gains ONE row, `WAIT [13](clamp)==0` at the entry phase, because the model's `Assembly_Station/Initialisation` owns Clamp `Home Finished->Home Pos` and the clamp is already assumed home, so the owned movement becomes a confirming WAIT rather than a command. No new motion — it is strictly safer than commanding `clamp=1` without confirming home first. **⚠️ NEW REPORT WARNING (not an error):** `Assembly_Station/Cover_PnP_returned` observes `CoverPnp_Gripper/AtReleasePos` but does not own that gripper; a jaw's reported states are the CAT's handshake, not the twin's stop numbering (R-12), so the observation is reported instead of silently dropped. The old code emitted nothing there either. **NEXT (user): regenerate `_vc` -> deploy -> Feed should now advance the Transfer and hand off, with Disassembly returning it after the bearing is clear.**

- **2026-07-26 (e) — ✅ RIG-CONFIRMED by the user. Full record: `Docs/COVER_SENSOR_FIRST_CYCLE_FIX.md`. COVER FIRST-CYCLE FIXED PROPERLY: ADDRESSED SENSOR REFRESH, "sample THEN report". Supersedes the (d) per-frame re-sample, which was only half a fix. CodeGen 0 err; DLL -> runner; all three models regenerate with `[Hcf][Validate] PASS` + `[Parity] PASS` + `[BX1][Scanner] OK`, 0 errors; two runs byte-identical (429 files, 0 differing); `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **WHY (d) WAS INSUFFICIENT (user caught it):** re-sampling on every inbound ring frame rescues only a WRONGLY-latched level. `Sensor_Bool`'s ECC has no same-level self-transition, so once the ECC holds the RIGHT level, no amount of re-reading emits anything. The actual boot sequence is: BX1's INIT chain is `FB1 -> TopCoverSensor -> covers -> BX1_IO`, i.e. the sensor samples an unwritten symlink FIRST (reads 0, latches `Sensor_FALSE`, publishes 0), then the broker INITs, decodes the word (07-25(m)) and drives `RD` -> re-sample -> TRUE != Sensor_FALSE -> **publishes 1**. So a correction IS emitted — but it is a ONE-SHOT emitted during BX1 INIT, and if M580's ring is not yet live it is lost; the ECC now sits in `Sensor_TRUE` and every later re-sample is silent. Deploying all three together makes it a coin flip. **AND the naive `StateHandling.CNF -> StateHandling.REQ` shortcut is WRONG (user's catch):** `updateComponentState.REQ` publishes the CACHED `state_sts` (fed from `FB1.Status`), so publishing from the same event that starts the sample is a fan-out race that can report the pre-sample value — on an active-low input that briefly reports the wrong level and can release the cover sequence early. **THE CONTRACT NOW IMPLEMENTED — addressed request -> fresh sample -> exactly one report -> WAIT evaluates:** (1) `Sensor_Bool` gains `RPT` (event in), `SMP` (event out) and one transient `Arm` state whose only action is `SMP`; arcs `START|Sensor_TRUE|Sensor_FALSE -> Arm [RPT]` and `Arm -> START [1]`. Parking the ECC in **START** is the whole trick: from START both level transitions are unconditionally available, so the sample that `SMP` triggers ALWAYS fires one and therefore ALWAYS emits `CNF` — that is what makes an UNCHANGED level report, with no flag, no gate and no algorithm. (2) `Sensor_Bool_CAT` wires it strictly SERIALLY, never fanned out: `StateHandling.CNF -> FB1.RPT -> FB1.SMP -> FB2.REQ -> FB2.CNF -> FB1.REQ -> FB1.CNF -> StateHandling.REQ`. The published value is the one just read. (3) `ProcessCompiler.EmitCondition` emits `CMD <sensor>` immediately before every Control.xml-derived sensor WAIT. **WHY IT CANNOT STORM (proven from the ECCs, not asserted):** `StateHandling.CNF` is emitted ONLY by `updateComponentState`'s `BREQ` state, entered only when `component_state_in.dest_name = name`; the `REQ` algorithm sets `component_state_out.dest_name := ''`, so **no report can ever trigger any sensor** — only a frame that names the component. One request in, one report out, ZERO traffic while idle. The `BREQ_PASS` state (frames for someone else) emits `BCNF` only, never `CNF`. And the free-running I/O-scan push (`FB2.CNF -> FB1.REQ`, which every HCF-bound sensor depends on) keeps its change gate untouched — this is exactly why a blanket same-level self-transition was REJECTED: it would have made every scan push publish. **⚠️ CASE-SENSITIVITY TRAP HIT AND FIXED (the 07-25(c) lesson, second time):** the refresh must address the sensor by its **verbatim component name**, NOT `TemplateMap.RingKey`. Actuators are claimable in lower case because the injector lower-cases `actuator_name`; a `Sensor_Bool_CAT` is parameterised with `name` verbatim, so a lower-cased target circles the ring unclaimed. `SyslaySysresParityValidator` CAUGHT this on the first build (`'Assembly_Station' commands 'topcoversensor', which no component answers to`) — the guard added on 07-25(c) paid for itself. The validator's claimable set now also accepts `name` (what `BREQ` actually compares), which makes it strictly more accurate. **VERIFIED on all three models:** `RPT`/`SMP`/`Arm` + all 4 arcs present, XML well-formed; `FB2.REQ` driven by exactly `['RD','StateHandling.INITO','FB1.SMP']`; publish still solely `FB1.CNF -> StateHandling.REQ`; the (d) per-frame wire RECONCILED AWAY (a deployed tree self-heals); the cover gate reads `… CMD TopCoverSensor=0 · WAIT[6]==1` (`_se` row36, `_sw5` row35) and `… CMD TopCoverSensor=0 · WAIT[16]==1` (`_vc` row34); every Control.xml sensor gate refreshed (Assembly BearingSensor+ShaftSensor+TopCoverSensor, Feed PartInHopper), 0 unrefreshed; recipes grow by EXACTLY the refresh rows (`_se` 16/54/63 -> 17/57/63, `_sw5` 16/53/61 -> 17/56/61, `_vc` 20/52/64 -> 21/55/64); all prior fixes intact (BX1 INIT decode, no cyclic tick, 4 FiveState INIT arcs, ring latch clear). **⚠️ NOT DONE, and why — "Option 2" (reorder the BX1 INIT chain so the broker inits before the sensors) is NOT IMPLEMENTABLE as stated:** `PLC_RW_BX1` exposes **no `INITO`**, so it cannot be spliced into the INIT chain, and fanning `FB1.INITO` to both broker and sensor guarantees no ordering. Its INTENT is already met by a better mechanism: the broker's INIT-time word decode (07-25(m)) fires `CoverSensorEvent` -> `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.REQ` -> `TopCoverSensor.RD` -> re-sample, so a stale INIT sample is corrected at boot regardless of chain order. Adding an `INITO` to a shared broker type for no extra guarantee was judged not worth the risk. **⚠️ SMALL COSTS, stated not hidden:** each refresh also fires `FB1.CNF -> MqttFmt.REQ`, so one extra MQTT publish per sensor gate per cycle; and the Mapper-injected material-bridge pair (`WAIT PAA==0 · WAIT PAA==1`) is deliberately NOT refreshed — it wants a genuine 0->1 edge, and refreshing it would only establish the 0. **NEXT (user): regenerate -> deploy -> a cover already sitting in place must be picked up on cycle 1, with no take-out-and-back-in, regardless of the order the three controllers come up.**

- **2026-07-26 (d) — [SUPERSEDED BY (e)] COVER SENSOR "TAKE THE COVER OUT AND IN": a level that was ALREADY TRUE before the PLC started never produced an edge, and the whole publish chain is edge-only. ONE connection. CodeGen 0 err; DLL sha256 7F23DE66 -> 5 locations; all three models regenerate with PARITY PASS; `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **ROOT CAUSE — three change gates in series, no re-assert anywhere:** (1) `BX1_IO.CoverSensorEvent` (the broker's `changeEventM262_2`) fires ONLY when input-word bit5 flips; (2) it REQs `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover`, whose `CNF` fires `TopCoverSensor.RD` -> `FB2.REQ` (re-sample); (3) `Sensor_Bool`'s ECC has NO same-level self-transition — `Sensor_TRUE` is entered only from `START` or `Sensor_FALSE` — and `FB1.CNF -> StateHandling.REQ` is the ONLY publish trigger in the CAT. So the sensor publishes exactly once per genuine bit5 transition and never otherwise. At INIT the CAT samples `Input` BEFORE the broker has written it (`FB1.INITO -> TopCoverSensor.INIT` is first in the BX1 chain, `BX1_IO.INIT` is downstream of it) so it publishes a stale **0**; a cover already sitting there produces no later edge, and the one corrected frame — if it is emitted before the cross-device link is up — is simply lost. Nothing can re-announce an unchanged level, so `state_table[6].name` never gets the truth and the engine's validity gate correctly refuses `WAIT [6]==1` forever. **WHY IT ALWAYS WORKS FROM CYCLE 2 (the part that looked weird):** bit5 feeds TWO sinks — `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.VALUE1` (the cover sensor) AND `BX1IO_Sense_CoverPnp_Gripper.VALUE2` — it is the same physical bit. During cycle 1 the gripper grips and releases the cover, flipping bit5 twice; those are genuine transitions, so fresh frames land and the slot stays populated from then on. The machine's own cover handling repairs the startup race after the first pass. It was never a sensor fault or an intermittent signal. **THE FIX — one connection, `stateRprtCmd_in.CNF -> FB2.REQ`:** an inbound ring frame is the one trigger that PROVES the ring is alive, and routing it to the RE-SAMPLE (never to `StateHandling.REQ`) leaves the existing change gate in charge — `FB1` publishes only if the freshly read level disagrees with the state its ECC is already in. So the sensor corrects itself on the first frame after the broker has written the real value, then goes silent. **Self-limiting by construction:** its own frame comes back round the ring, finds the ECC already in agreement, and emits nothing — so it cannot self-amplify, which is exactly why `RD -> StateHandling.REQ` (the 07-25(k) storm) was the wrong shape and stays deleted. No timer, no poll, no cyclic publisher, and ZERO traffic while the line is idle; the re-sample ticks only in proportion to genuine ring activity. **VERIFIED on all three models:** `FB2.REQ` now driven by `['RD','StateHandling.INITO','stateRprtCmd_in.CNF']`, publish still solely `FB1.CNF -> StateHandling.REQ`, the `RD -> StateHandling.REQ` bypass still absent, XML well-formed, **PARITY PASS**; scope proof — a normalised whole-file diff of the generated `Sensor_Bool_CAT.fbt` against the live pre-fix one is **EXACTLY ONE ADDED LINE**. Applies to every `Sensor_Bool_CAT` instance, but only the broker-fed cover sensor has an `RD` driver at all; the HCF-bound sensors (PartInHopper, PartAtAssembly, BearingSensor, ShaftSensor) rely on the I/O-scan push and simply gain the same self-correction for free. **⚠️ RULED OUT, so do not re-chase:** `TopCoverSensor` (id 6) DOES collide with `Transfer` (id 6), but the two are on SEPARATE rings — the Assembly ring is `TopCoverSensor -> covers -> Assembly_Station -> Disassembly -> Ejector -> Robot -> PartAtAssembly -> BearingSensor -> M580 actuators -> Clamp -> back`, and Transfer sits on the M262-local Feed ring — so M580's `state_table[6]` is written only by the cover sensor and the gate is semantically correct (same benign shape as the documented slot-10 case). The cover sensor's report reaches Assembly_Station in 4 hops over one cross-device link, so transport was never the issue either.

- **2026-07-26 (c) — INIT WAS A DEAD END: a sensor-fitted five-state actuator that is not sitting exactly on a sensed stop at power-on could never leave INIT, so it never published and could never be commanded. Added two command-driven recovery arcs. CodeGen 0 err; DLL sha256 1C0EA65E -> 5 locations; all three models regenerate with PARITY PASS; `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **SYMPTOM (user, `_sw5`, after regenerating with 07-26(b)):** Assembly parked with `cmd_target_name='coverpnp_vr'`, `cmd_state=1`, `CMDREQ=15` — i.e. on `WAIT [15]==2` after `CMD coverpnp_vr=1`. Cover_vr had moved at deploy and returned home, then the line stalled at the cover block. **RULED OUT FIRST, from the deployed BX1 sysres (so these are not re-chased):** the cover sensor publishers all exist and carry the right symlinks (`BX1IO_Sense_CoverPNP_Vr` -> `BX1_RES.CoverPNP_Vr.athome/atwork`, fed from `BX1_IO.CoverPnpVrAtHome/AtWork`, REQ'd by the broker's `CoverPnpVrEvent` change detector); the coil write chain is complete and terminates correctly (`BX1_IO_Cycle.EO -> Coil_Hr.REQ -> CNF -> Coil_Vr.REQ -> CNF -> BX1_IO.REQ_INT_BOOL`, with `CoverPNP_Vr.OutputToWork -> Cover_Pnp_Vr_Q`); `CoverPNP_Vr` and `CoverPnp_Gripper` carry `RuleTable Count=0` so no interlock can swallow the command; BX1 has no station-adapter chain so `mode` takes the core's `InitialValue=1` and the launch guard's mode term is satisfied; the recipe's WAIT slot matches the commanded actuator (`coverpnp_vr` = id 15). **ROOT CAUSE:** 07-26(a) made the INIT classification strict and 07-26(b) made it sensor-aware, but INIT remained a state with only two exits — `AtHomeInit` (needs `athome`) and `AtWork` (needs `atwork`). A sensor-FITTED actuator sitting between stops at power-on, or one whose home sensor does not assert, satisfies NEITHER: the core stays in INIT, INIT emits `INITO` and not `pst_out` so it never publishes, `state_table[15].name` stays `''`, the engine's validity gate correctly refuses the WAIT — and because the launch arc is only reachable from `AtHomeInit`, the actuator can never be commanded out of it either. Deadlock by construction. The strictness itself is right (it is what stops an unknown position being reported as Home); the missing piece was any way OUT of "unknown". **THE FIX — two arcs, no new FB, no timer, no assumption:** `INIT -> ToWork [pst_event AND mode = 1 AND state_val = 1 AND toWorkInterlock = FALSE]` and `INIT -> ToHome [pst_event AND mode = 1 AND state_val = 3 AND toHomeInterlock = FALSE]`. So an actuator whose position is unknown does NOTHING at power-on — no motion, no invented state report — and is resolved by the first genuine recipe command, which drives it to a real stop where its own sensor then confirms arrival and it reports normally. Motion happens only on an explicit command, exactly as it would from `AtHomeInit`, so this adds no start-up hazard; deliberately NOT the Ground-Truth `INIT -> ToHome`-on-a-sensor-reading approach, which moves the arm at power-on and was removed on purpose in 07-25(h). Interlock terms are carried on both arcs so a blocked actuator is still blocked. **VERIFIED on all three models:** all four INIT arcs present, core and CAT XML well-formed, `homeSensorFitted` still wired, the seven-state swivel core/CAT do not reference `FiveStateActuator` at all so they are untouched, the full `_sw5` cover sequence is intact (`coverpnp_vr=1 · WAIT[15]==2 · coverpnp_gripper=1 · WAIT[16]==2 · …`), **PARITY PASS on all three**. **⚠️ STILL UNPROVEN ON THE RIG — state the honest limit:** this makes an unknown-position actuator commandable, so `CMD coverpnp_vr=1` will now drive it and the WAIT will satisfy once its atwork sensor confirms. If it STILL stalls after regenerating, the remaining cause is physical and outside the generator: watch `CoverPNP_Vr.InputHandler.atHomeOutput`/`atWorkOutput` and the broker's `BX1_IO.CoverPnpVrAtHome`/`CoverPnpVrAtWork` — if the coil energises (`Cover_Pnp_Vr_Q`) and the arm moves but `atwork` never asserts, that is the Vr work sensor, not the logic.

- **2026-07-26 (b) — REGRESSION I INTRODUCED, FOUND AND FIXED: the stricter INIT Home classification STRANDED EVERY SENSORLESS five-state actuator. `CoverPnp_Gripper` and `Ejector` could never leave INIT, never publish and never accept a command. CodeGen 0 err; DLL sha256 E66D8EBD -> 5 locations; all three models regenerate with PARITY PASS; `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **SYMPTOM (user, `_sw5` on the rig):** cover_gripper does nothing. **ROOT CAUSE — mine, from 2026-07-26 (a):** that change made the arc `INIT -> AtHomeInit [athome = TRUE AND atwork = FALSE]` so an unknown/mid-travel position could not be mistaken for Home. But `athome` is NOT the raw DI — it is `InputHandler.atHomeOutput` from `No_Sensor_Handler`, whose outputs are LATCHED and start FALSE (its INIT algorithm is `;`). For a SENSORLESS actuator the only thing that can set `atHomeOutput` is `toHomeTimerEvent`, and that timer is gated `FB12 = AND(outputToHome, NOT HomeSensorFitted)` — it starts only on a home COMMAND. So at boot a sensorless actuator has `athome = FALSE` with nothing able to change it, the core sits in INIT forever, never publishes (INIT emits `INITO`, not `pst_out`), so `state_table[id].name` stays `''` and the recipe's WAIT blocks — and since the launch arc is only reachable from `AtHomeInit`, it can never be commanded either. **BLAST RADIUS (from the deployed sysres, not inference):** `_sw5` and `_se` = `CoverPnp_Gripper` + `Ejector`; `_vc` = those two plus `Bearing_Gripper` and `Shaft_Gripper` (four). Every one carries `HomeSensorFitted=FALSE`. **THE FIX — the requirement was conditional and I implemented it unconditionally:** "may report Home at startup only when its Home sensor confirms it" presupposes a home sensor EXISTS. Where none is fitted there is nothing to confirm with and the timer model IS the declared truth. So the classification is now sensor-aware: new `FiveStateActuator` InputVar `homeSensorFitted : BOOL`, sampled on `INIT` and `input_event` alongside `athome`, and the arc becomes `INIT -> AtHomeInit [(athome = TRUE OR homeSensorFitted = FALSE) AND atwork = FALSE]`. `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` wires the existing per-instance `HomeSensorFitted` parameter straight to it. **DEFAULT IS SAFE BY CONSTRUCTION:** a BOOL InputVar defaults FALSE = "no home sensor" = the permissive pre-existing behaviour, so any instance whose wire were ever missing degrades to the old semantics rather than stranding. `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` is the only CAT instantiating `FiveStateActuator`, and it is wired. The `INIT -> AtWork` arc is untouched: `atwork` is FALSE at boot for a sensorless actuator, so it can never wrongly claim Work. **VERIFIED on all three models:** arc and wire present in every generated tree; per-instance classification is now `HomeSensorFitted=TRUE -> requires athome=TRUE (sensor-confirmed)` and `FALSE -> assumes Home at INIT (timer-modelled)`, listed actuator by actuator; the full cover sequence is intact in `_sw5` Assembly (`coverpnp_vr=1 · WAIT[15]==2 · coverpnp_gripper=1 · WAIT[16]==2 · … · coverpnp_hr=3 · WAIT[14]==0`); the seven-state swivel artefacts do not reference `FiveStateActuator` at all so they are untouched; regenerated core and CAT are XML well-formed in all three; **PARITY PASS on all three**. **LESSON, recorded so it is not repeated:** `athome`/`atwork` at the core are the HANDLER's latched outputs, never the raw DIs — any ECC condition written against them must account for the sensorless (timer-modelled) case, where they are FALSE until a motion command runs a timer.

- **2026-07-26 — TWO-POSITION (five-state) SWIVEL SUPPORTED, MODEL-DRIVEN: a Bearing_PnP the twin models with only Work1 + Work2 and no centre stop now binds its I/O through the Five_State CAT's own Home/Work vocabulary. CodeGen 0 err 0 warn; DLL sha256 E6714E68 -> 5 locations; all THREE models generate clean with PARITY PASS; `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed/deployed.** **WHAT WAS ALREADY CORRECT (verified, not changed):** `TemplateMap.ResolveActuatorCatType` already selects the CAT from the twin's state graph — Seven only when `stateCount == 7 || IsBranchedSevenState`, and `IsBranchedSevenState` keys on PARALLEL+ALTERNATIVE transition STRUCTURE, not names — so `_sw5`'s 5-state Bearing_PnP already resolved to `Five_State_Actuator_CAT`. The recipe was already right too: `ActuatorGraph`'s position folding is applied ONLY to the centre-home CAT, so a five-state actuator's State_Number IS its stop, and `_sw5`'s twin numbers its stops canonically (`AtWork1`#0 @181, `ToWork2`#1, `AtWork2`#2 @0, `ToWork1`#3, `AtWork1Finished`#4 @181) — a 1:1 match to the five-state runtime (AtHomeInit/ToWork/AtWork/ToHome/AtHome). Generated commands are `CMD=1 · WAIT==2 · CMD=3 · WAIT==0`, i.e. **Work2 -> Work command, Work1 -> Home command**, exactly the required mapping, with no recipe change. Interlocks emit in the normal five-state form (`FilterToCentreHomeRange` is seven-state-only). **THE ONE REAL GAP — I/O binding:** `M580SymbolBinder` chose the swivel's channel map in a STATIC CTOR gated on `MapperConfig.StubSevenStateActuatorsAsFiveState`, a dead simulator-era test flag (`= false`). So a genuine five-state swivel still got seven-state ports and 4 of its 5 channels dangled (`atwork1`, `atWork2`, `OutputToWork1`, `OutputToWork2` are not ports on `Five_State_Actuator_CAT`, which exposes only `athome`/`atwork`/`OutputToWork`/`OutputToHome`). A dangling symbolic fails the EAE compile — the same class as the 2026-07-06 Clamp split-brain. **THE FIX (net +69 lines / 3 files, superseded code deleted not layered):** the flag-gated static ctor is REPLACED by `SwivelChannels(catType)`, resolved per generation from the DEPLOYED FB Type — which is itself what `SystemLayoutInjector` wrote from `ResolveActuatorCatType`, so the twin's state graph stays the single source of truth and nothing keys off a model name. New DRY sibling helper `HcfBindingSupport.BuildComponentTypeMap` (mirrors the existing `BuildComponentIdMap`) reads it back. Two-position mapping: `SwivelArmAtPick` (Work1 @181, the rest position) -> **`athome`**, `SwivelArmAtPlace` (Work2 @0, the working position) -> **`atwork`**, `Swivel_Arm_Left_Q` -> **`OutputToHome`**, `Swivel_Arm_Right_Q` -> **`OutputToWork`**, and the centre sensor `SwivelArmAtHome` -> **blanked** (a two-position swivel has no centre stop; blanking reuses the existing missing-component path, since a dangling symbolic is what breaks the compile). Seven-state branch is byte-for-byte the old else-branch. Added `TemplateMap.SevenStateCentreHomeCat` so the CAT name is one constant rather than a repeated literal. **VERIFIED — all three models generated into `scratchpad/cat`, live tree mtime unchanged:** `_sw5` Bearing_PnP = `Five_State_Actuator_CAT`, `Target (Work1:=2, Work2:=0, Home:=4)`, RuleCount=4, HCF `DI00=athome DI01=atwork DO17=OutputToHome DO18=OutputToWork`, **0 dangling pins, 0 unbound Swivel symbolics, 0 unmapped channels**, recipe `CMD=1 · WAIT==2 · CMD=3 · WAIT==0` in BOTH processes; `_se` and `_vc` **unchanged** — Seven CAT, `Target (2,4,6)`, HCF `atwork1/atWork2/athome/OutputToWork1/OutputToWork2`, Assembly `1->2, 3->4, 5->0` and Disassembly's reverse `3->4, 1->2, 5->0`. A full binding-set diff shows **every non-swivel channel identical across `_se` and `_sw5`** (Shaft_Hr/Vr/Gripper, Bearing_Gripper, Clamp, both sensors) — the swivel is the only delta, and `_sw5` binds 21 channels vs `_se`'s 22, the difference being exactly the blanked centre sensor. `[Parity] PASS` on all three. **NOTE:** `StubSevenStateActuatorsAsFiveState` still exists and is still `false`; its remaining uses (CAT selection gate, the forced-sensorless branch in `SystemLayoutInjector`, a UI display gate) are a separate coherent "stub everything as five-state" test mode, NOT superseded by this change — only its I/O-binding use was, and that is the code that was deleted. `_sw5`'s swivel keeps `WorkSensorFitted=TRUE`/`HomeSensorFitted=TRUE` and now has both sensors genuinely bound. **⚠️ R-12 UNCHANGED:** which physical coil drives to Work1 vs Work2 (`Swivel_Arm_Left_Q`/`Right_Q`) is still rig-confirm-before-motion; the two-position mapping follows the same Left=Work1 / Right=Work2 convention the seven-state branch already assumed.

- **2026-07-25 (n) — SHAFT_HR NEVER REPORTING HOME FIXED: restored the actuator input Poll that the rig-proven Ground Truth carries. This WAS a regression from removing the ring storm — the storm had been standing in for the Poll. CodeGen 0 err 0 warn; DLL sha256 01973577 -> 5 locations; `_se` + `_vc` regenerate clean (7.2s / 6.9s); `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed at write time.** **SYMPTOM:** Assembly parked on row `[32] WAIT [10]==0` with `cmd_target_name='shaft_hr'`, `cmd_state=3`, `CMDREQ=15` — the arm was physically home but never reported it. Row `[24] WAIT [10]==2` (to work) HAD satisfied in the same run. **ROOT CAUSE (settled by Ground-Truth diff, not inference):** `FiveStateActuator`'s arrival arcs carry NO event term — `ToWork -> AtWork [atwork = TRUE]`, `ToHome -> AtHome [athome]`, `AtHome -> AtHomeInit [atwork = FALSE AND athome = TRUE]` — so they are evaluated only when SOME event reaches the core. The sensor path is `Inputs.CNF -> InputHandler.inputEvent -> InputHandler.CNF -> FB8 -> FB1 -> ActuatorCore.input_event`, and `Inputs` is a `SYMLINKMULTIVARDST`, i.e. **sample-on-REQ**. In the current build `Inputs.REQ` has **NO driver at all**, so nothing re-samples. The motion timers are NOT a fallback: `FB11 = AND(outputToWork, NOT WorkSensorFitted)` and `FB12 = AND(outputToHome, NOT HomeSensorFitted)` gate them, and Shaft_Hr has BOTH flags TRUE, so neither timer ever runs for it. **THE GROUND-TRUTH DIFF IS DECISIVE:** `Demonstrator_20260617-150925279_WorkingEndtoEnd` — the build that ran the line end to end — carries `<FB ID="55" Name="Poll" Type="E_DELAY" …><Parameter Name="DT" Value="T#200ms" /></FB>` in `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` with `INIT -> Poll.START`, `Poll.EO -> Poll.START` and **`Poll.EO -> Inputs.REQ`**. The later GT (2026-07-20) and the current build have NO Poll and NO `Inputs.REQ` driver. So between those builds the only thing re-evaluating the un-evented arrival arcs was the 20 Hz ring event storm, which reaches every actuator core as `StateHandling.BCNF -> InterlockManager.REQ_WORK1 -> CNF_WORK1 -> ActuatorCore.Ilck_event`. Removing that storm in 07-25(k)/(l) was correct, but it took the substitute clock with it — hence shaft_hr stranding in `ToHome`. **THIS ALSO REFUTES the transition-ordering theory** (that `No_Sensor_Handler`'s `START->AtWork` being declared before `START->AtHome` strands the handler when both DIs read TRUE): both GT archives carry that handler ECC **byte-identical**, same declaration order, and the 06-17 build homed shaft_hr fine with no storm — so ordering cannot be the cause. Not pursued further. **THE FIX:** `ActuatorCatTemplatePatcher.EnsureFiveStateInputPoll`, called from `TemplateLibraryDeployer` beside the other Five_State CAT patches. Re-creates GT's Poll exactly (E_DELAY, `DT = T#200ms`, `INIT -> START`, `EO -> START` self-loop, `EO -> Inputs.REQ`); idempotent, and it verifies the `Inputs` FB exists before wiring. Because `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` is force-re-extracted every deploy, the pristine template's max FB ID is 54, so the Poll lands as **ID 55 — the same ID Ground Truth uses.** **WHY THIS DOES NOT REVIVE THE STORM:** the Poll is strictly LOCAL — it re-reads a symlink and re-evaluates an ECC. The ring report is driven by `ActuatorCore.pst_out`, which fires only on a genuine ECC state entry, so an actuator sitting still publishes NOTHING. Idle ring traffic stays zero and `ProcessEngine.state_change`/`SCNF` stay still. The cost is a local 5 Hz tick on each actuator's `Inputs.REQ`/`CNF` counters — the same trade already accepted for the cover sensor's re-read, and exactly what the working build did. **NOT applied to the centre-home swivel** (GT has no Poll there either — `StripCatHomeSensorPoll` removed it deliberately) **nor to `Sensor_Bool_CAT`** (its broker-fed instance already re-reads via `RD`; adding a Poll there would re-introduce the 07-25(l) tick). **⚠️ NOTE ON A PRIOR WARNING:** the 2026-07-10 entry warned that an E_DELAY `<Parameter Name="DT">` inside a CAT causes `ERR_XML_UNKNOWN_TAG`. GT 06-17 contains exactly that element inside `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` and compiled and ran, so that warning does not apply to this form; the 07-06 error must have had another cause. This patch reproduces GT's element verbatim. **VERIFIED ON BOTH MODELS** (real hidden runner, `--output-root` into `scratchpad/vrf6`; live CAT mtime unchanged): Poll present exactly ONCE with `DT=T#200ms` and all three connections in both; swivel CAT still has NO Poll; `Sensor_Bool_CAT` still has no Poll and still no `RD -> StateHandling.REQ`; the 07-25(m) cover INIT decode still present; **recipes byte-identical** (`_se` 16/54/61, `_vc` 20/52/62); `updateComponentState` INIT still `state_cmd := 0;`; **syslay==sysres PARITY PASS in both**. Scope: normalised whole-file diff of the CAT is +6 lines (the Poll FB, its DT parameter, and the three connections); the MqttFmt/MqttPub lines in the diff are a reordering artefact, content identical. **NEXT (user): regenerate -> deploy -> shaft_hr should now report home and Assembly should advance past row [32] into the cover block. `state_change`/`SCNF` must STILL sit still while idle — if they climb again, something is publishing on the ring, not merely re-reading.**

- **2026-07-25 (m) — COVER PRESENT-AT-BOOT FIXED: the EtherNet/IP input word is now decoded at INIT, so a cover already in place is reported without a physical remove-and-replace. ONE connection added. CodeGen 0 err 0 warn; DLL sha256 4933B535 -> 5 locations; `_se` + `_vc` regenerate clean (11.9s / 10.6s); `C:\Demonstrator` NOT touched. NOT committed at write time.** **SYMPTOM:** after the 07-25(l) tick fix the cover gate still needed the cover physically taken out and put back. **MECHANISM (verified directly, and it CORRECTS the 07-25(l) entry):** `EIPInputs_Bool` (WordToBits) computes `bit0..bit15` ONLY on `REQ`, and its sole REQ driver is `EIP_Input_Word.CNF`, which fires from the composite `REQ` i.e. from the scan. The change detector IS already clocked at INIT (`EIP_Input_Word.INITO -> FB2.Scan_Event`) but it is handed UNDECODED bits, so `CoverPnpSensor` reads FALSE against a `_PreVal` that is also FALSE, no change is seen, and the cover level is not announced at INIT at all. The correction only arrives on the first 50 ms scan — and because both gates then latch (`_PreVal` TRUE in the broker, ECC in `Sensor_TRUE` in the sensor) that is the ONLY frame that will ever exist. So the 07-25(l) claim that the detector "DOES produce the event" at power-on was right about the first SCAN and wrong about INIT. **THE FIX:** add `EIP_Input_Word.INITO -> EIPInputs_Bool.REQ`. `INITO` genuinely carries the sampled word — `SYMLINKMULTIVARDST.fbt` declares `INITO With QO, STATUS, VALUE${I}` and its doc states "Each 'INIT' causes an 'INITO'-event" — so decoding on INITO hands the detector the real bit5 while `_PreVal` is still FALSE, and a cover already in place fires `CoverSensorEvent` at INIT. Bounded by construction: once per INIT, nothing added to the scan, so it cannot reintroduce the per-scan ring publish removed in 07-25(k)/(l). Order-safe either way round the INITO fan-out — if `FB2.Scan_Event` is evaluated first it sees the old all-FALSE bits and updates no `_PreVal` (the algorithm assigns only inside its own IF), then the decode's `CNF` re-clocks it with the real ones. **⚠️ WIRED IN THE RIGHT PLACE — the first attempt was in dead code:** `EmbedCoverBridgeInComposite` is gated on `MapperConfig.Bx1BridgeInsideComposite`, which is **FALSE** — BX1 runs the EXTERNAL bridge (`BX1IO_Sense_TopCover` / `BX1IO_Coil_*` / `BX1_IO_Cycle` in the sysres), so that method never runs and a patch inside it is inert. The broker INTERNALS (`EIP_Input_Word`, `EIPInputs_Bool`, `FB2`) are in the composite in BOTH modes, so the patch is applied from `TemplateLibraryDeployer` beside `DeployArtifact("PLC_RW_BX1")` via `Bx1IoBrokerInjector.EnsureInitWordDecodeInComposite`, which is idempotent and reconciles onto an already-deployed composite (`PLC_RW_BX1` is copy-if-absent). It also self-checks that `EIPInputs_Bool` and `EIP_Input_Word` are present before wiring. **SCOPE PROOF — a normalised whole-file diff of the generated `PLC_RW_BX1.fbt` against the live pre-fix one is EXACTLY ONE ADDED LINE** (138 -> 139): `<Connection Source="EIP_Input_Word.INITO" Destination="EIPInputs_Bool.REQ" />`. **VERIFIED ON BOTH MODELS** (real hidden runner, `--output-root` into `scratchpad/vrf5`; live composite mtime unchanged): wire present exactly ONCE in both; `INITO -> FB2.Scan_Event`, `EIPInputs_Bool.CNF -> FB2.Scan_Event`, `REQ -> EIP_Input_Word.REQ` and the injected `CoverFailsafe` all still present; the 07-25(l) cyclic wire still ABSENT and the 07-25(k) CAT bypass still ABSENT (no regression); `updateComponentState` INIT still `state_cmd := 0;`; swivel INIT arcs still `[('AtHomeInit','1')]`; **recipes byte-identical** (`_se` 16/54/61, `_vc` 20/52/62) with bearing `1,3,5` / `3,1,5`; **syslay==sysres PARITY PASS in both**. **⚠️ HONEST RESIDUAL — do not oversell this:** it makes the power-on report EARLY and TRUTHFUL, but the report is still a ONE-SHOT. If M580 is deployed or restarted AFTER BX1 (routine during recipe iteration), `state_table[6].name` reverts to `''` while BX1 stays latched, and Assembly row [33] `WAIT [6]==1` is dead until a physical 1->0->1 on input-word bit 5. A genuine re-assert is NOT cheap: firing `StateHandling.REQ` from `stateRprtCmd_in.CNF` is self-amplifying, and firing it from a free-running scan is exactly the storm just removed. Until a bounded once-per-cycle re-announce is designed, **the operating rule is: deploy all three together, or deploy BX1 LAST.** **⚠️ SEPARATE, UNCHANGED HARDWARE GAP:** `EIPInputs_Bool.bit5` feeds BOTH `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.VALUE1` (TopCoverSensor.Input) AND `BX1IO_Sense_CoverPnp_Gripper.VALUE2` (CoverPnp_Gripper.atwork) — the same bit. So slot 6 means "the cover gripper is holding a cover", while Assembly row [33] gates on it BEFORE row [36] `CMD coverpnp_gripper=1` tells the gripper to grip. That gate is circular and no generator change can fix it: it needs either a real cover-present DI wired to a spare coupler input, or the `TopCoverSensor/On` condition dropped from the twin.

So the residual climb was CAT-LOCAL, not ring traffic. **MECHANISM:** `BX1_IO_Cycle` (E_DELAY, `DT = T#50ms`) drove BOTH `BX1_IO.REQ` (the word read — required) AND `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.REQ` (the cover publisher). The publisher's `CNF` fires `TopCoverSensor.RD`, and `RD -> FB2.REQ` re-reads the CAT — so the sensor CAT was ticked 20×/s forever even though 07-25(k) had correctly stopped it PUBLISHING. **WHY THE CYCLIC WIRE IS PURE REDUNDANCY (proven from the broker, and it overturns the 07-21 rationale):** the publisher's other driver is `BX1_IO.CoverSensorEvent`, which comes from `FB2` = `changeEventM262_2` inside `PLC_RW_BX1`. The cyclic wire never addressed the actual gap and, once `RD` existed, became a permanent 20 Hz tick. **THE FIX:** `Bx1IoBrokerInjector` is the sole writer of these connections; its `AddEvent(ScanFbName.EO -> tcSrc.REQ)` is replaced by a RECONCILE that REMOVES that connection, so a tree already deployed with it self-heals. **KEPT deliberately (all verified present in both regenerated models):** `BX1_IO.CoverSensorEvent -> SRC.REQ` (the change path — now the only publisher trigger), `SRC.CNF -> TopCoverSensor.RD` (the CAT re-read, now fired only on a real change), `CoverPnp_Gripper.INITO -> SRC.INIT` (boot), **`BX1_IO_Cycle.EO -> BX1_IO.REQ`** (the 50 ms word read — LOAD-BEARING, it is what clocks the change detector; removing it would blind the covers), `BX1_IO_Cycle.EO -> BX1IO_Coil_*.REQ` (the coil write chain — outputs must stay scan-driven), and the `BX1_IO_Cycle.EO -> .START` self-loop. Net: the broker still reads and writes I/O at 20 Hz, the change detector still evaluates at 20 Hz inside the broker where it is free, but the sensor CAT is touched ONLY when the cover bit actually changes. **SCOPE PROOF — a normalised whole-file diff of the generated BX1 sysres against the live pre-fix one is EXACTLY ONE REMOVED LINE:** `<Connection Source="BX1_IO_Cycle.EO" Destination="BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.REQ" />`. **VERIFIED ON BOTH MODELS** (real hidden runner, `--output-root` into `scratchpad/vrf3`; the temp root is copied FROM the live tree which still HAS the cyclic wire, so this is also a genuine self-heal test; live BX1 sysres mtime unchanged): wire ABSENT in both, all six kept connections present in both, the 07-25(k) CAT fix still holds (`RD -> StateHandling.REQ` absent), the 07-25(j) latch clear still holds (`INIT = state_cmd := 0;`), swivel INIT arcs still `[('AtHomeInit','1')]`, **recipes byte-identical** (`_se` 16/54/61 rows, `_vc` 20/52/62), bearing gating unchanged (`_se` first Work cmd row 6, `_vc` row 4; `1,3,5` Assembly / `3,1,5` Disassembly), **syslay==sysres PARITY PASS in both models**. **NEXT (user): regenerate -> deploy -> `TopCoverSensor` FB2.REQ/FB2.CNF/FB1.REQ must now sit still and step only when a cover is physically placed or removed; `StateHandling.REQ` should stay at its boot value until then. If a cover-present gate ever hangs after a cold start with the cover already in place, the suspect is the broker's first-scan change event, NOT this wire — check `BX1_IO.CoverSensorEvent` fires once at boot.**

`_vc` at row 4 behind its merged-ring transport), bearing `1,3,5` Assembly / `3,1,5` Disassembly, phase chain intact. **WHY THE STORM PHYSICALLY MOVED THE SWIVEL — AND ONLY THE SWIVEL (found by the verification workflow, then verified directly):** every relayed ring frame drives `StateHandling.BCNF -> CommonInterlockManager.REQ_WORK1` AND `-> REQ_WORK2` (`Seven_State_Actuator_Centre_Home_CAT.fbt:337,345`), whose `CNF_WORK1`/`CNF_WORK2` both drive `ActuatorCore.ilck_event` (`:341,344`). `ilck_event` is an EVENT INPUT to the swivel core, and the core's launch arc is `AtHomeInit -> ToWork1 [((mode=1 AND state_val=1) OR (mode=2 AND state_val=1) OR (mode=3 AND toWork1Setup)) AND homeWork1Interlock = FALSE]` — **NOT ONE disjunct carries an event qualifier**, so in IEC 61499 it is re-evaluated on every arriving input event. The storm therefore re-tested the swivel's work-launch guard **20 times a second**. The contrast is exact and is the whole discriminator: `FiveStateActuator.fbt:92` gates the SAME arc as `((pst_event AND mode=1 AND state_val=1) OR (pst_event AND mode=2 AND state_val=1) OR (setup_event AND mode=3 AND toWorkSetup)) AND toWorkInterlock = FALSE` — **every disjunct carries an event qualifier**, so no amount of `ilck_event` kicking can move a five-state actuator. A climbing `Assembly_Station.state_change` can therefore ONLY come from BX1. (This also settles a long-standing open question in this file: EAE demonstrably DOES bridge the cross-device stateRprtCmd adapter at runtime — the user's own Watch counter is the proof.) **GROUND-TRUTH COMPARISON CONFIRMS BOTH HALVES ARE REGRESSIONS:** the 2026-06-17 GT has NO `RD` event on `Sensor_Bool_CAT` at all (its 200 ms Poll drove only `FB2.REQ`), and the 2026-07-20 GT has `RD -> FB2.REQ` ONLY with `BX1_IO_Cycle.EO` never wired to `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.REQ`. Neither GT contains either half of the chain; both halves exist only in the current build, added 07-21 and 07-23. **RESIDUAL LOGIC ROUTE, REPORTED NOT SILENTLY CLOSED:** the third disjunct `(mode = 3 AND toWork1Setup)` needs NO `state_val` and NO ring frame — `toWork1Setup` comes from `IThis.toWork1`, the HMI faceplate — so in Setup/jog mode (mode 3) an event kick alone could launch the arm. The rig runs mode 1 (Automatic; the user's Watch shows `Mode = 1`), so this is inert as operated. **The SevenState arcs were AGAIN assessed and deliberately NOT changed:** adding a `pst_event` term would give the swivel the same edge discipline as every five-state actuator, but Ground Truth carries the same level-based arcs, the storm that weaponised them is now gone, and `state_cmd` is cleared at INIT by 07-25(j) — so with `state_val = 0` and mode 1 the guard cannot fire. Changing it would diverge the CAT from GT on an unsafe rig with no way to test. Recorded as a recommendation, not applied. **⚠️ VERIFICATION HONESTY:** the workflow's 6 read agents and its diagnosis agent completed, but ALL 8 adversarial-refutation agents FAILED on the account weekly limit, so the adversarial pass did NOT run and its "survived review" result is vacuous. Removing the bypass restores the pre-07-23 behaviour, which is what ran on the rig. If a cover gate is ever seen hanging after a cold start with the cover already in place, that is this race — the correct remedy is a bounded re-assert tied to the ring coming up, NOT restoring an unconditional per-scan publish. **NEXT (user): regenerate from VueOne -> deploy all three -> in EAE Watch `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.state_change` and `SCNF` must now sit STILL while the line is idle and tick only when something actually changes state. If the swivel still moves at start-up with the counters stable, the remaining path is the physical output fallback (see 07-25(j)), not the logic.**

- **2026-07-22 — ARCHIVED .sln FAILED TO UNARCHIVE ("Unable to import topology / Internal Server Error") — FIXED permanently.** EAE's Archive packs ONLY files registered in `TopologyManager.topologyproj`, and `BroadcastDomain_Default Network.json` (written by `BroadcastDomainEmitter.Emit`) was never registered — only domains that `EnsureReferencedDomains` *created* were. If you ever see the `[Interlock][Guard] ... ABORTED` message at Generate, it means a CAT `.fbt` was locked — close EAE and re-Generate; the retry usually avoids it entirely.**

- **2026-07-10 — TOP-COVER SENSOR MQTT FIX (Mani: TopCoverSensor publishes only state 0; bearing/shaft publish both). It's a state-REPORTING gap, software-fixable. Applied to BOTH the Mapper (gate-verified, 6 commits PUSHED de35a97) AND the live Demonstrator (sysres+syslay+CAT direct-edit + dfbproj touched). CodeGen+MapperUI 0 err; gate all validators PASS.** **DIAGNOSIS (traced deployed artefacts):** TopCoverSenosr (BX1, Sensor_Bool_CAT) froze at 0 because (a) nothing fed its Input and (b) Sensor_Bool_CAT samples its Input ONLY at INIT (FB2's single trigger, no re-read). M262/M580 sensors don't show it — their HCF-bound DIs are I/O-scan event-driven (re-fire on change); BX1's input comes via the SOFTWARE broker (no I/O event), so a broker-fed passive sensor freezes at boot. The cover-detect signal EXISTS: input-word **bit5 = BX1_IO.CoverPnpSensor** (already the gripper's grip-detect, `CoverPnp_Gripper.atwork`) — the ONLY cover-present bit the coupler carries. **FIX (2 parts):** (1) a 1-BOOL broker SRC `BX1IO_Sense_TopCover` (`SYMLINKMULTIVARSRC_1559B0FF8170C9BA0`) publishes bit5 -> `BX1_RES.TopCoverSenosr.Input`, boot-INIT'd + REQ'd on `BX1_IO.CoverSensorEvent` (bit5 change); (2) a SCOPED re-read EVENT input **RD -> FB2.REQ** added to `Sensor_Bool_CAT`, fired by the SRC's CNF (`BX1IO_Sense_TopCover.CNF -> TopCoverSenosr.RD`). Sensor_Bool's ECC is CHANGE-GATED (CNF only when Input flips) so it publishes ONLY on change. **⚠️ LANDMINE CAUGHT:** first tried an E_DELAY Poll with `<Parameter Name="DT">` -> that is EXACTLY the 2026-07-06 `ERR_XML_UNKNOWN_TAG` (a CAT FBType.FBNetwork.FB.Parameter is rejected; the SYSRES allows it, the CAT does NOT). Reworked to the RD event (NO E_DELAY, NO DT). RD is UNWIRED on M262/M580 sensor instances -> zero behaviour change there (gate-proven: M580 has 0 RD wires). **MAPPER (durable):** `Bx1IoBrokerInjector` (external-bridge branch, finds the Sensor_Bool_CAT whose name contains "cover") + `SwivelCatPatcher.EnsureSensorBoolReadEvent` (adds RD event input; wired after StripCatHomeSensorPoll). **DEPLOYED (immediate test):** direct-edited BX1 sysres + syslay (SRC + RD-trigger) + Sensor_Bool_CAT.fbt (RD event) + touched IEC61499.dfbproj — all XML well-formed; EAE re-saved the sysres with layout hints (accepted the SRC). **GATE-PROVEN:** CAT EventInputs=[INIT,RD], RD->FB2.REQ, 0 E_DELAY/DT; SRC+RD-trigger in regenerated sysres+syslay; M580 sensors unchanged; HCF+PARITY+CONNECTIVITY+EMBEDDED-MQTT+COVER-SAFE PASS exit 0. **SEMANTIC:** bit5 is the gripper grip-detect, so TopCoverSenosr now mirrors "a cover is held" (the real cover-detect, but tracks the gripper — there is NO independent top-cover-magazine bit wired). **NEXT (user): EAE is RUNNING -> Reload Solution (or close/reopen) so it re-reads the edits -> Build (watch: the RD event on Sensor_Bool_CAT is the one interface change; if the _HMI companion flags it, report it) -> Deploy BX1 -> Login -> place/remove a cover -> `smc/topcoversenosr` publishes 0 AND 1. For an INDEPENDENT top-cover sensor (not the gripper grip), a physical DI must be wired to a spare coupler input + the broker taught to unpack that bit.**

- **2026-07-07 (b) — NO-CLAMP MATERIAL GATE RE-ADDED (user: after feeder pushes the part, Bearing_PnP must not go to AtWork1 until PartAtAssembly=TRUE, not just Transfer-settled). CodeGen+MapperUI+gate 0 err; no-clamp 5 validators PASS; clamp gate BYTE-IDENTICAL; MapperUI relaunched PID 186932; 1 file PUSHED (HEAD==origin/main 7b9a345).** **CHANGE (`AssemblyRecipe`, MergeFeedRing-gated):** the fresh-edge fix (7-07a) had DROPPED the `WAIT(PartAtAssembly=1)` material gate on the theory the Transfer edge subsumed it — the user wants the physical part-present confirmation kept. Re-added it AFTER the Transfer edge so the no-clamp Assembly head is now `assembly_idle -> WAIT(Transfer=Advancing=1) -> WAIT(Transfer=Advanced=2) -> WAIT(PartAtAssembly=1) -> bearing_pnp=1`. Order is deliberate: the edge's advance-start(1) is a transient that must be caught FIRST (PartAtAssembly-first would miss it, since PartAtAssembly only goes TRUE ~when Transfer is already Advanced), and a settled transport alone doesn't prove a part is present (could settle empty). **DRY:** both the material-gate resolution (via `HandoffPlanner.AssemblyStart` — WaitId>=0 = the rig synth sensor id 3 from `Config/smc-rig.yml`; the twin does not model PartAtAssembly, so it's an explicit rig material-presence SAFETY gate, not a recipe hack) is now ONE local `EmitMaterialGate()` reused by the clamp path (emitted first) and the no-clamp path (after the edge). `TryGetComponentId("PartAtAssembly")` does NOT resolve it (synth sensors aren't in `allComponents`) — that's why HandoffPlanner is the correct resolver. **GATE-PROVEN:** no-clamp head = `assembly_idle=0 -> WAIT(6,1) -> WAIT(6,2) -> WAIT(3,1) -> bearing_pnp=1`; Bearing_Gripper WorkSensorFitted=FALSE (untouched); 0 Clamp FBs; 5 validators PASS. - **2026-06-22 — TELEMETRY_CAT BROUGHT BACK, FIXED PROPERLY (user: "bring that mechanism back, adjusting it properly"). The wrapper now compiles because the struct-member connections that EAE rejected are gone — replaced by two trivial helper Basic FBs. CodeGen+MapperUI+gate 0 err, MapperUI relaunched PID 35332. That's why I'd set `useTelemetryCat:false` (which `SweepTelemetryCat` then DELETED the deployed copy — the source ZIP `Template Library/Composite/Telemetry_CAT.zip` was always intact in git `fbca427`; only the C:\Demonstrator copy was swept). **THE PROPER FIX (no member connections):** two new helper Basic FBs do the struct split in ST (legal + precedented — the recipe engine reads `Recipe[i].StepType` the same way): **`TelemetryUnpack`** (`Config:TelemetryConfig` in → 6 scalars `QI/ConnectionID/URL/ClientIdentifier/ValidateCert/CACert`, ST `QI := Config.QI;`…) + **`TelemetryPack`** (6 status scalars in → `Health:TelemetryHealth` out). Rewired `Telemetry_CAT.fbt`: `Config →(whole)→ Unpack.Config`; `Unpack.{6 scalars} →(scalar)→ Conn.{6 inputs}`; `Conn.{6 status} →(scalar)→ Pack.{inputs}`; `Pack.Health →(whole)→ Health`. Events serialized: `INIT→Unpack.REQ→Unpack.CNF→Conn.INIT` (scalars ready before Conn samples QI/ConnectionID at INIT, URL/etc persist for CONNECT); `Conn.CONN_STATE_IND→Pack.REQ→Pack.CNF→CONN_STATE_IND` (Health refreshed on state change); INITO/CONNECTO/DISCONNECTO pass through. **EVERY connection is now whole-struct or scalar→scalar — ZERO member-level connections.** **CHANGED (5 files, each committed separately):** NEW `Template Library/Basic/TelemetryUnpack/IEC61499/TelemetryUnpack.fbt` + `TelemetryPack/…/TelemetryPack.fbt`; rebuilt `Template Library/Composite/Telemetry_CAT.zip` (forward-slash entries, IDCounter=4, 3 internal FBs); `TemplateLibraryDeployer` deploys the 2 helpers in the `UseTelemetryCat` branch (+ force-refresh delete of the 3 .fbt so a re-deploy picks up new wiring) and `SweepTelemetryCat` removes them on flag-off; `telemetry.yml useTelemetryCat:false→true`. **GATE-PROVEN:** all 4 validators PASS (HCF+PARITY+CONNECTIVITY+EMBEDDED-MQTT), EXIT 0; the 3 instances emit (Telemetry_M262/M580/BX1, URL/ConnectionID=SMC/ClientId unique, ValidateCert=0); deployed `Telemetry_CAT.fbt` has Unpack/Conn/Pack + **0 member connections** + whole-struct Config→Unpack.Config & Pack.Health→Health; Unpack ST=6 `Config.` reads, Pack ST=6 `Health.` writes; all 6 type files (2 .fbt + 2 .dt + composite + offline.xml) registered in the dfbproj. Telemetry INIT + INITO→CONNECT wired per resource (CONNECTIVITY pass). **NEXT (user): CLOSE EAE → Generate IEC61499 Code (re-deploys Telemetry_CAT + the 2 helper FBs + datatypes) → reopen EAE → Build → THIS is the final gate (the _gate verifies generation, not the EAE compile). - **2026-06-10 — FIXED M262 "all I/O red": the M262 sysres had EVERY component FB DUPLICATED (Feeder ×2 etc.) → EAE saw ambiguous instances → all M262 I/O red. Added a dedup to `SysresFbMirror.MirrorFbsIntoSysres`. CodeGen+MapperUI 0 err, relaunched (PID 51364). Committed + PUSHED per user request.** Root cause: an M262 component's instance id flip-flops between regens — a sysres FB ID is `ComputeMirrorId(syslayId)` = the syslay id with its top hex bit flipped (60AE…↔E0AE…). When the syslay id flips, the mirror's add/update loop (whose `existingByName` index holds only ONE element per name) updates one copy but leaves the previous-id copy behind → the component is declared TWICE with ID/Mapping swapped (ID=60AE/Map=E0AE **and** ID=E0AE/Map=60AE). The `.hcf` binds just one `{res}.{id}.{pin}` (the syslay/application id, resolved via the FB Mapping) so EAE can't disambiguate → red. **Fix:** before the add loop, drop any same-named sysres FB whose `Mapping` is NOT a CURRENT syslay FB id; the loop then keeps exactly one per name and HcfPatchService re-aligns the .hcf to the survivor. Name-scoped to syslay components (FB1/FB2/non-mirrored FBs untouched), so M580/BX1 unaffected. **VERIFIED** by a throwaway harness running the REAL `MirrorFbsIntoSysres` on the actual dup'd M262 sysres: BEFORE=27 FBs all-duplicated → AFTER=DUPLICATES **NONE**, Feeder kept as ID=60AE/Mapping=E0AE (=the .hcf id). **NOTE:** my Stage 4 changes did NOT cause this (the mirror is shared, the flip is pre-existing, exposed by the many regens today); it's a general robustness fix. **Next handoff:** user Test Runtime → the regenerated M262 sysres has ONE FB per component → EAE Reload → M262 I/O resolves (no longer red). (The id flip still causes a cosmetic "new instance" each regen; the dedup guarantees single+aligned every time, so I/O always resolves — a deeper id-stability fix is a separate follow-up.)

The OLD `InjectSimSwivelForce` reads data pins `current_state1/2_to_plc` and fires off `plc_out` and matches `Type=="Seven_State_Actuator_CAT"` — the Centre-Home CAT has NONE of those (zero composite OutputVars; coils published only as symlinks `'$${PATH}OutputToWork1/2'` by the `Output` FB; no boundary coil-change event). So synthesis must be a symlink BRIDGE: a `SYMLINKMULTIVARDST_277E97BEC1451D2C` (2-BOOL — CONFIRMED present in the deployed lib, DST/SRC share the hash) subscribing to `<Res>.<name>.OutputToWork1/2` → its CNF drives a `SYMLINKMULTIVARSRC_277E97BEC1451D2C` publishing `<Res>.<name>.atwork1`/`atWork2`. **DEFECT in the naive coil-mirror (why I did NOT ship it blind):** the core's `AtHomeEnd` algorithm (the only one that runs in the `AtHome` ECState) sets `current_state:=6` but does NOT clear the coils, and `toHome` energizes the OPPOSITE coil to swing through center — so at `AtHome` a coil is still TRUE → a level mirror holds an `atwork` TRUE → `AtHome→AtHomeInit` (needs both atwork FALSE) never fires → recipe stalls at the final home-wait (pnp=0). - **2026-06-02 — Fixed the bearing pick-place recipe LOGIC: gripper now CLOSES at Pick, OPENS at Place (was opening both times). Committed `e5dd168`, pushed.** Root cause (source-proven): `Bearing_Gripper` deploys as `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` (TemplateMap.ResolveActuatorCatType — Robot/5-state/not-branched → Five_State), and the Assembly twin uses the SAME direction-agnostic WAIT condition `Bearing_Gripper/ReturnedHome` (Control.xml State_Number 0) for BOTH the grip step (Assembly state `Gripping_Part`) and the release step (`BearingPnPOpenGripper`). So the condition-driven classifier's generic rule `condCmdState = (waitState==1||2) ? 1 : 3` collapsed BOTH gripper commands to `3` (toHome/open) → gripper opened twice, never gripped → swivel carried nothing. Fix in `ProcessRecipeArrayGenerator.ClassifyState` (condition-driven Five_State branch): for a gripper target (`IsGripperTarget` — name contains gripper/grasp) derive the command from the Assembly STEP NAME via new `MapGripperCommandFromStepName` (open/release/unclamp → cmd 3 open; grip/grasp/clos/hold/pick → cmd 1 close; open checked first so `BearingPnPOpenGripper` routes OPEN while `Gripping_Part` routes CLOSE). Non-gripper Five_State targets (clamps, cylinders) keep the condition-derived command, so Feed_Station + others are byte-identical. New Assembly bearing recipe: `bearing_pnp=1 (Pick) → bearing_gripper=1 (CLOSE/hold) → bearing_pnp=3 (Place) → bearing_gripper=3 (OPEN/release) → bearing_pnp=5 (Home) → END`. Gripper holds through the swivel-to-place move (commanded close at step 2, untouched until open at step 6). Auto-retract unaffected (gripper now advanced+retracted, swivel cmd3=Place counts as retract). M580SymbolBinder maps gripper closed=atwork / open=athome / single OutputToWork coil → cmd1 energises the close valve = convention-correct & sim-correct. **R-12 caveat:** if the rig grips/releases backwards (energise OutputToWork OPENS it), that's a coil/sensor wiring inversion → swap in M580SymbolBinder, NOT the recipe. CodeGen builds 0 errors; harness NOT yet re-run (MapperUI PID 44236 locks CodeGen.dll → MapperTests can't rebuild). The C check only asserts CMD-row presence (not the value), so the change is harness-safe.

Without this the swivel never leaves START on a Pick/Place/state_val=Home command no matter what the ring delivers.
  2. **New post-processor `SimulatorPostProcessor.InjectSimSwivelForce`** (~200 lines, added to `CodeGen/Services/SimulatorPostProcessor.cs`). For every `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` instance in the SIM syslay AND sysres it injects one top-level `SimSwivelForce_<name>` SYMLINKMULTIVARSRC publishing `'<ResourceName>.<name>.atwork1'` and `'<ResourceName>.<name>.atwork2'`, with VALUE1/2 data-wired from the actuator's own `current_state{1,2}_to_plc` outputs and REQ event-wired from `<name>.plc_out`. Net: atwork1 closes the instant the actuator drives coil 1, mirror for coil 2. ECC's ToPick→AtPick / ToPlace→AtPlace gates fire in sim without physical sensors. `athome` left unpublished (defaults FALSE; the ECC's INIT→START falls through on the atworks=FALSE arm and AtHome is timer-driven). Idempotent re-runs, deterministic IDs (path-independent SHA1 seed). MainForm_simulator now calls it after the no-sensor override; harness also calls it.
  3. **Flipped `StubSevenStateActuatorsAsFiveState = false`.** Bearing_PnP now deploys as `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` (verified: B4 reports `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` for Bearing_PnP, `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` for the other Assembly actuators). The recipe's MapSevenStateCommandFromConditionName emits state_cmd 1/2/0 for pick/place/home in lock-step with the CAT's TargetPick/Place/HomeState=1/2/0 parameters.
- **Harness coverage extended:** B4 now computes the expected CAT type per-actuator (Bearing_PnP → Seven_State_Actuator_CAT when stub off, Five_State_Actuator_CAT otherwise). New `D2` check verifies SimSwivelForce wiring for every Seven instance in both syslay and sysres. F3 fixed — `CheckRepeatability`'s second-run pipeline now mirrors the main pipeline by also calling `InjectSimSwivelForce`.
- **Result:** harness 18/18 green. **NOTE:** the stub flip also affects the **rig path** — if Test Runtime is clicked, Bearing_PnP will deploy as Seven_State on M580. That's intentional (fixes a/b/c prepare for it) but unverifiable until the rig comes back. Sim-only commitment held throughout.

## Convergent findings from the May 29 deep investigation

**Do not re-investigate these. They are source-proven; cite line numbers if you need to verify.**

1. **The bearing rig outputs read 1970 timestamp not because of the `.hcf` binding form** (`M580SymbolBinder.cs` emits Form-1 GUID triples, byte-identical to the working M262). The deployed `bin\Deploy\` was purged by `CompileCachePurger` on every Generate; a download done *before* the last Generate is already stale by the time the user tests.

2. **The `Process1_Generic.fbt` interface has no data/event outputs at all** — `EventOutputs = [INITO]`, `OutputVars = empty`, only the two adapter plugs (`stateRptCmdAdptr_out`, `stationAdptr_out`). So all four wires in `SystemLayoutInjector.WireSevenStateActuators` (line 538-541) are phantom — `proc.state_update`, `proc.actuator_name`, `proc.state_val` and `proc.{lc}` do not exist on the Process side. The Process commands actuators only over the `stateRprtCmd` ring.

3. **`updateComponentState.BREQ` (lines 82-89)** matches `IF component_state_in.dest_name = name` (case-sensitive STRING `=`). On match it sets `state_cmd` and fires `CNF`. Shared by Five_State and Seven_State — so the recipe targeting works identically for both.

4. **The Seven_State swivel's deeper fault (decisive):** `SevenStateActuator2`'s Pick/Place/Home transitions gate on `process_state_name = actuator_name AND state_val = TargetPickState/...`. The ring delivers `state_val` + `pst_event` via `StateHandling.CNF` but **does not deliver `process_state_name`**. A `grep` of the Mapper shows `process_state_name` is referenced only at lines 523 and 539 (both phantom wires) and is **never `SetParam`'d**. So on a deployed Seven instance `process_state_name` is the STRING default `''` while `actuator_name = 'bearing_pnp'` → gate FALSE forever → only the bare `tohome` event moves it. The recipe's `state_val` arm is already in lock-step (recipe emits `pick=1/place=2/home=0` via `ProcessRecipeArrayGenerator.MapSevenStateCommandFromConditionName` at lines 1248-1261; CAT defaults match at `SystemLayoutInjector.BuildMinimalActuatorParameters` lines 1664-1668).

5. **The deploy-revert trap:** `ExtractToEae` is copy-if-absent. `DemonstratorWiper.FoldersToDelete` includes `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` explicitly. A Clean → next Generate re-extracts the committed `fecba79` no-socket zip, which then sticks. **The surgical Seven_State CAT in the deployed `.fbt` is not in the committed `.cat.zip`**, so it dies on a Clean. This is irrelevant to the simulator focus (sim uses Five_State stub for bearing) but matters when the rig comes back.

6. **`BuildStation2Wiring` (sys-lay) vs `ResourceWireEmitter` (sysres) disagree on Seven_State adapters.** Syslay hardcodes Five_State and wires `Bearing_PnP.stationAdptr_in/out`; the deployed Seven_State CAT has no stationAdptr port. Throws on import when Seven is deployed. Same story: only matters when the rig comes back.

### Deferred rig fixes — ALL LANDED 2026-05-30

These were the three fixes that make the rig Seven_State path work. **All three landed in the 2026-05-30 session and are verified by the harness.** The stub flag is now FALSE; Bearing_PnP deploys as the real `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` in both sim and rig paths. Kept here as a reference for where each landed:

1. **Commit surgical `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT.fbt` into the committed `.cat.zip`.** Landed: `Template Library/CAT/Seven_State_Actuator_CAT.cat.zip` — `diff -q` against the deployed copy is empty (19 ring/StateHandling tokens). DemonstratorWiper's Clean + re-extract path no longer reverts the surgery.
2. **`BuildStation2Wiring` excludes Seven from `stationChain`.** Landed: `SystemLayoutInjector.cs` ~lines 2230-2240. Mirrors `ResourceWireEmitter.NoStationAdapterTypes` (sysres side); the two halves of the wiring layer now agree about Seven's adapter set.
3. **`BuildMinimalActuatorParameters` parameterises `process_state_name = <lowercased name>` on Seven instances.** Landed: `SystemLayoutInjector.cs` ~lines 1664-1668. Statically satisfies `SevenStateActuator2`'s ECC name-gate so Pick/Place/Home commands actually fire under the ring model.

Plus the new piece this session built on top: `SimulatorPostProcessor.InjectSimSwivelForce` (sim sensor synthesis for Seven_State). See Status entry above.

**Caveat for the rig path:** the stub flip also takes effect on the hardware path (`btnTestStation1_Click`). The rig is currently unsafe so this is unverified; coil-direction must be physically confirmed before any rig deploy (`Docs/REVERTED_FIXES.md` R-12).

The headless harness asserts these. Each iteration aims to turn red items green.

### A. Generation succeeds end-to-end

- [ ] `FinalizeM262Stack` completes without exception.
- [ ] `M262SysresWireEmitter.Emit(cfg, report)` completes.
- [ ] `HcfPatchService.PatchDeployed(cfg, path, bindings, report)` completes.

### B. Syslay + sysres have the right shape

- [ ] One `Process1_Generic` FB named `Assembly_Station` (M580 process) is present in the SIM syslay.
- [ ] One `Process1_Generic` FB named `Disassembly` is present.
- [ ] The Process FB named `Feed_Station` (M262) is present.
- [ ] All Assembly actuators are present: `Bearing_PnP`, `Bearing_Gripper`, `Shaft_Hr`, `Shaft_Vr`, `Shaft_Gripper` (`Clamp` is NOT a Component in the current `Full_System_Fixture.xml` — see iteration 3 audit). **Type depends on stub flag**: under `StubSevenStateActuatorsAsFiveState=false` (current), `Bearing_PnP` is `Type=Seven_State_Actuator_CAT`, the others are `Type=Five_State_Actuator_CAT`. Under stub=true, all are Five_State. The harness's B4 check computes the expected type per-actuator from the live flag.
- [ ] Every `Five_State_Actuator_CAT` instance in the syslay AND sysres has `WorkSensorFitted="FALSE"` and `HomeSensorFitted="FALSE"` (no-sensor override).
- [ ] No FB references a source pin that doesn't exist (phantom checks: `proc.state_update`, `proc.actuator_name`, `proc.state_val` should NOT appear as `Source=` in `EventConnections`/`DataConnections` of the SIM syslay).

### C. Assembly recipe has actuator coverage

- [ ] `Assembly_Station.Recipe` parameter has at least one `CMD` row (`StepType=1`) per Assembly actuator (`bearing_pnp`, `bearing_gripper`, `shaft_hr`, `shaft_vr`, `shaft_gripper`, `clamp`), not just bearing.
- [ ] Recipe terminates in an `END` row (`StepType=9`).
- [ ] Every `Wait1Id` in Assembly's recipe resolves to an FB id present in the SIM resource (no out-of-scope drops the harness silently swallows).

### D. Sim no-sensor model is intact

- [ ] `SimHopperForce` SYMLINKMULTIVARSRC is present in both syslay and sysres.
- [ ] Its wiring matches `SimulatorPostProcessor.InjectSimHopperForce`'s defensive idempotent rebuild (FB1.INITO → SimHopperForce.INIT → Area.INIT; PartInHopper.INITO → SimHopperForce.REQ).
- [ ] `VerifySimActuatorsNoSensorOrAbort` would PASS (every Five_State has the no-sensor params, no duplicates).

### D2. Sim sensor synthesis for Seven_State (added 2026-05-30)

- [ ] For every `Seven_State_Actuator_CAT` instance in the SIM syslay AND sysres, a `SimSwivelForce_<name>` SYMLINKMULTIVARSRC FB is present (currently 1: `Bearing_PnP` → `SimSwivelForce_Bearing_PnP`).
- [ ] Each is wired: event `<actuator>.INITO → SimSwivelForce_<name>.INIT`, event `<actuator>.plc_out → SimSwivelForce_<name>.REQ`, data `<actuator>.current_state1_to_plc → VALUE1`, data `<actuator>.current_state2_to_plc → VALUE2`. Net: `atwork1`/`atwork2` close the instant the coil energises.
- [ ] No-op-green when `StubSevenStateActuatorsAsFiveState=true` (no Seven instances to wire).

### E. Disassembly is parked

- [ ] `Disassembly.Recipe` is a single `END` row (the intra-PLC handoff park guard). This is fine — Assembly is the focus.

### F. Repeatability

- [ ] Harness runs deterministic: same Control.xml + same MapperConfig → byte-identical generated `.syslay`/`.sysres`/`.hcf` two runs in a row.

## How to verify a behaviour-preserving change (the byte-identical gate)

`MapperTests` runs 199 active tests (the `SimulatorEndToEndHarness` was deleted
2026-06-16; what runs now is the compiler-boundary, metamorphic, negative-fixture
and target-profile suites). They prove PLAN semantics, not bytes: prove a change
preserves generation with the `_gate` byte-identical
Demonstrator diff — it reads a fixed base (`C:\_gate\base`), generates into
`C:\_gate\work`, and NEVER writes the live `C:\Demonstrator`:

```powershell
# From C:\VueOneMapper
dotnet build _gate\Gate.csproj -c Debug
_gate\bin\Debug\net10.0\gate.exe C:\_gate\snap_pre     # before the change
# ...make the change, rebuild _gate (recompiles CodeGen)...
_gate\bin\Debug\net10.0\gate.exe C:\_gate\snap_post    # after the change
# then SHA256-diff snap_pre vs snap_post — must be byte-identical
```

The gate also runs HcfReferenceValidator + SyslaySysresParityValidator +
MqttConnectionValidator and exits non-zero on a split-brain or parity divergence.

## EAE verification steps (Test Simulator era — OBSOLETE)

> ⚠️ **OBSOLETE (2026-06-16).** Describes the removed Test Simulator EAE flow (the
> "harness green" precondition and the Test Simulator button are gone). For the
> current rig flow: generate via **Test Runtime**, then in EAE Reload Solution →
> clean Build → Deploy → Login. Kept below as historical context.

The harness proves the generation pipeline produces the right artefacts. EAE is the only thing that proves the *runtime* actually cycles. Sequence (once per change to the generator):

1. **Close MapperUI** if it's open. CodeGen.dll is loaded into MapperUI's process — running MapperUI must be restarted before it sees a new build.
2. **Rebuild MapperUI** in Visual Studio (or `dotnet build MapperUI/MapperUI/MapperUI.csproj`). This recompiles CodeGen too.
3. **Launch MapperUI**, Browse to the Control.xml you want to drive the sim with (e.g. `C:\VueOne\system\SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes\Control.xml`).
4. **Click "Test Simulator"** (NOT Test Runtime — that's the rig path, currently unsafe).
   - The activity log will show the same steps the harness exercises, plus the live `[Simulator][SymCheck]` and `[Simulator][Verify]` lines that confirm SimHopperForce wiring and the no-sensor override.
5. **In EAE 24.1:**
   - Reload Solution (so EAE picks up the regenerated `.syslay` / `.sysres` / `.hcf`).
   - In the device tree, set the dPAC's **Active Network Profile to "Local Test"** (this is what "sim mode" means in EAE — there is no SIM_RES resource type; the resource stays `EMB_RES_ECO` and the simulator runtime is selected by the network profile).
   - **Clean Build** (not a partial build — every Mapper regen purges `bin\Deploy\` and `obj\` via `CompileCachePurger`, so an incremental build is undefined).
   - **Deploy.**
   - **Login** to the dPAC. EAE Online Watch becomes active.
6. **Add this Watch list** to verify the Assembly cycle:
   - `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.CurrentStep` — should march from 0 through the recipe.
   - `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.cmd_target_name` — the lowercased actuator the engine is currently commanding.
   - `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.cmd_state` — the state value being sent. Under stub=false (current): Bearing_PnP/Seven_State takes 1=pick, 2=place, 0=home; Five_State actuators (Bearing_Gripper, Shaft_*) take 1=toWork, 3=toHome.
   - `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.CMDREQ` — pulses when a CMD is issued.
   - For each Five_State Assembly actuator (`Bearing_Gripper`, `Shaft_Hr`, `Shaft_Vr`, `Shaft_Gripper`): the FB's `current_state_to_process` (internal `FiveStateActuator` output) — should advance 0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → 0 over the recipe's CMD/WAIT cycles, driven by the no-sensor timer.
   - For `Bearing_PnP` (Seven_State): watch the FB's `state` STRING (cycles `INIT` → `moving` → `atPick` → `toPlace` → `atPlace` → `timerstart` → `ToInitPos` → `timerstop` → `stopAtHome`) and `current_state{1,2}_to_plc` (the coil-drive BOOL outputs `SimSwivelForce` reads to synthesise `atwork1`/`atwork2`).
   - `Feed_Station.PartInHopper.Input` — should read TRUE at startup (SimHopperForce keeps it forced).
7. **Expected sequence on a successful run** (intra-process Assembly only — cross-process Wait1Ids onto Feed_Station get dropped in sim per `SystemLayoutInjector.ClassifyState`'s out-of-scope guard, so Assembly runs standalone):
   - `cmd_target_name` cycles through `bearing_pnp` → `bearing_gripper` → `shaft_hr` → `shaft_vr` → `shaft_gripper` (and the BX1 cover chain). Each actuator's `current_state_to_process` advances on its own toWorkTime/toHomeTime via the No_Sensor_Handler timer.
   - `Assembly_Station.ProcessEngine.CurrentStep` increments past each CMD/WAIT pair until it reaches the END row (StepType=9), at which point it parks (or loops, depending on engine config).
   - `Disassembly.ProcessEngine.CurrentStep` stays at the single END row (intra-PLC handoff park guard).
8. **If the recipe stalls at a row**, copy `CurrentStep`, `cmd_target_name`, `cmd_state`, and the `current_state_to_process` of the named actuator into the loop's next iteration prompt — that's enough to triage the stall from source.

**Fixture used:** `MapperTests\TestData\Full_System_Fixture.xml` (a copy of `C:\VueOne\system\SMC_Vue2VC_With_Processes\Control.xml`, 34 components — full SMC system with Bearing_PnP 13-state, Shaft_Hr/Vr, the Robot grippers, and the 3 Processes). Swapped in at iteration 2 (2026-05-29) because the older `Feed_Station_Fixture.xml` had only 8 Components (Feed-only — the 39 Assembly/Bearing/Shaft tokens were inside the Process FBs' transition names, not standalone Component definitions).

**Real Control.xml** lives at `C:\VueOne\system\Control.xml` and can be pointed at by the harness for one-off cross-checks, but the loop's deterministic iteration uses the committed `Full_System_Fixture.xml`. The legacy `Feed_Station_Fixture.xml` is kept in the repo for narrower Feed-only tests but the SimulatorEndToEndHarness no longer loads it.

## Standing rules

- **Commit each file separately.** No bundling. No `Claude` attribution in any commit message.
- **HTTPS push only**, never SSH. Don't touch `git config` or `~/.git-credentials`.
- **Push to `github.com/easensoy/Mapper`.**
- **PUSH AFTER EVERY UPDATE — automatically, without being asked (user directive 2026-06-15).** The moment a change is complete (any code edit, fix, or Status update), commit each changed file separately (own message, no attribution) and `git push origin main` IMMEDIATELY. Do NOT batch multiple changes, and do NOT wait for the user to say "push" — every iteration must end with the work on GitHub. Still never `git add -A` (the MQTT certs/keys + throwaway `_dis`/`_verify2` harnesses must stay out): stage only the specific files you changed. After pushing, verify `git rev-parse HEAD` == `git rev-parse origin/main`.
- Never regenerate `MapperTests\TestData\SMC_Rig_IO_Bindings.xlsx` — it is hand-crafted per-CAT content.
- **After every code change, Claude itself builds → kills MapperUI → relaunches it** (kill→build→relaunch when the DLL is locked). Do NOT hand this off to the user — they should find an already-running, up-to-date MapperUI and just click Test Simulator/Test Runtime. See `BUILD_RELAUNCH.md`. Still note in the Status what was rebuilt.
- Before claiming a checklist item green, the harness must actually assert it. Don't mark it on confidence alone.
